**Summary:**

The paper argues that current alignment strategies for large language models (LLMs) are fundamentally inadequate to prevent misuse due to their "shallow alignment." Despite efforts to instill norms like helpfulness, honesty, and harmlessness, LLMs are vulnerable to adversarial attacks that exploit conflicts between these norms. The author contends that LLMs lack a genuine capacity for normative deliberation, leading to easily exploitable behavioral dispositions. Drawing from moral psychology, the paper contrasts this with human resilience to similar attacks, which is attributed to explicit deliberation. The author argues that these limitations carry significant implications for AI safety and regulation, suggesting current approaches are insufficient to mitigate potential harms from increasingly capable AI systems. The paper further explores how even reasoning-focused LLMs are susceptible, and proposes a novel kind of "thought injection attack" demonstrating how LLMs can be manipulated to generate reasoning traces that promote harm, even as the final answer is withheld.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper offers a valuable perspective by applying concepts from moral psychology and social engineering to the problem of LLM alignment. The critique of "shallow alignment" and the emphasis on normative conflicts provide a nuanced understanding of why adversarial attacks are so effective. The introduction of the "thought injection attack" is also novel and concerning. The analysis is particularly pertinent given the increasing emphasis on RLMs and chain-of-thought reasoning.
*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical issue in AI safety: the vulnerability of LLMs to misuse, especially through cleverly designed adversarial prompts. The finding that even RLMs are not immune, and potentially introduce new vulnerabilities, carries significant weight. The implications for AI safety and regulation are clearly articulated, highlighting the limitations of current approaches.
*   **Strengths:** The paper presents a clear and well-reasoned argument, supported by empirical examples and references to relevant literature. The analogy between social engineering and prompt injection is insightful. The discussion of normative conflicts as a key vulnerability is compelling. The paper is written in a lucid style, making it accessible to a broad audience. The author's consideration of potential objections is thorough.
*   **Weaknesses:** While the paper effectively identifies the problem, it offers only general directions for addressing it, such as the need for explicit normative deliberation. The paper could benefit from a more in-depth exploration of potential technical solutions, perhaps drawing from areas like argumentation or formal ethics. Also, it largely focused on certain attacks related to napalm for demonstration. Considering other harmful scenarios such as misinformation spread with high confidence, etc. might strengthen the generality of the conclusion.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a substantial contribution to our understanding of the challenges of LLM alignment. It provides a compelling explanation for the effectiveness of adversarial attacks, highlighting the limitations of current alignment strategies. While it doesn't offer a complete solution, it identifies a critical problem and provides valuable directions for future research. The novelty of the "thought injection attack" and the detailed examples are significant. The limitations, namely the lack of specific solutions and narrower focus on attack domains, temper the overall impact.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### MARS: Radio Map Super-resolution and Reconstruction Method under Sparse Channel Measurements
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04682v1)
- **Authors**: Chuyun Deng, Na Liu, Wei Xie, Lianming Xu, Li Wang
- **Abstract**: Radio maps reflect the spatial distribution of signal strength and are essential for applications like smart cities, IoT, and wireless network planning. However, reconstructing accurate radio maps from sparse measurements remains challenging. Traditional interpolation and inpainting methods lack environmental awareness, while many deep learning approaches depend on detailed scene data, limiting generalization. To address this, we propose MARS, a Multi-scale Aware Radiomap Super-resolution method that combines CNNs and Transformers with multi-scale feature fusion and residual connections. MARS focuses on both global and local feature extraction, enhancing feature representation across different receptive fields and improving reconstruction accuracy. Experiments across different scenes and antenna locations show that MARS outperforms baseline models in both MSE and SSIM, while maintaining low computational cost, demonstrating strong practical potential.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "MARS: Radio Map Super-resolution and Reconstruction Method under Sparse Channel Measurements" introduces a deep learning-based method called MARS for reconstructing high-resolution radio maps from sparse channel measurements.  It leverages a combination of Convolutional Neural Networks (CNNs) and Transformers in a multi-scale architecture. CNN-based residual blocks extract local features, while a lightweight Transformer block captures global dependencies. The paper also includes a High-Frequency Filtering Module (HFM) to enhance feature extraction and a Pyramid-Aware UBlock (PAU) for multi-scale feature fusion. The loss function is designed to be building-aware, mitigating the impact of building shape reconstruction errors. Experimental results demonstrate that MARS outperforms baseline methods in terms of MSE and SSIM, while maintaining low computational cost.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Problem Relevance:** The paper addresses a crucial problem in wireless communication: efficiently constructing accurate radio maps with limited measurements. This has direct implications for smart cities, IoT, and wireless network planning.
    *   **Methodological Innovation:** The combination of CNNs and Transformers in a multi-scale architecture is a reasonable approach to capture both local and global features, which is essential for radio map reconstruction. The incorporation of the HFM module and the building-aware loss function are also potentially beneficial. The PAU is a clever way to take into account the different scales of RF signal propogation.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The paper presents experimental results on a standard dataset (RadioMapSeer) and demonstrates improvements over several baseline methods. The comparison across different experimental settings (different transmitter locations and unseen environments) strengthens the validation. The ablation study of the various components of the method are also solid.
    *   **Practical Potential:** The paper emphasizes low computational cost, indicating a potential for real-world deployment.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Incremental Novelty:** While the combination of CNNs and Transformers is not entirely novel *in general*, the specific architecture and its application to *radio map reconstruction* is important. It is inspired by other image SR tasks which diminishes it a little bit. How MARS adapts image SR for its specific need is novel though.

    *   **Dataset Limitations:** The RadioMapSeer dataset is based on simulations. While simulations are useful, performance on real-world measurements might differ significantly. The paper should acknowledge this limitation.
    *   **Limited Baseline Comparison:** The baseline comparison is adequate, but could be strengthened by including more recent state-of-the-art SR methods specifically designed for image inpainting or super-resolution with irregular sampling.

    *   **Overclaiming Generalization**: It claims generalization by testing on unseen transmitter locations *within known environments* and a testing on *unseen environments* respectively. The authors should be more careful with the phrasing and not overclaim the model is generalizable. These are essentially two specific, though different, kinds of overfitting which should be improved.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The paper contributes a practical and effective method for radio map reconstruction from sparse measurements.
    *   The combination of CNNs and Transformers and other specific modules such as the HFM and PAU modules demonstrates a promising direction for addressing the challenges of wireless channel modeling.
    *   The work provides a basis for future research on more robust and generalizable radio map reconstruction techniques.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign a score of **7**. The paper tackles an important problem with a well-motivated and reasonably innovative approach. The combination of CNNs and Transformers is not entirely new *in the general image SR community*, but is novel for its specific application to radio map reconstruction in the wireless communications community. The incorporation of building-aware loss and multi-scale architecture demonstrate a reasonable design process. While there are a few incremental innovations, it has potential. The experimental results are solid, and the discussion emphasizes practical considerations. The limitations related to dataset and baseline comparison lower the score slightly. The score reflects its relative importance and novelty in the realm of radio map reconstruction while also acknowledging that the work does not completely revolutionize the field.
Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### MMRefine: Unveiling the Obstacles to Robust Refinement in Multimodal Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04688v1)
- **Authors**: Gio Paik, Geewook Kim, Jinbae Im
- **Abstract**: This paper introduces MMRefine, a MultiModal Refinement benchmark designed to evaluate the error refinement capabilities of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs). As the emphasis shifts toward enhancing reasoning during inference, MMRefine provides a framework that evaluates MLLMs' abilities to detect and correct errors across six distinct scenarios beyond just comparing final accuracy before and after refinement. Furthermore, the benchmark analyzes the refinement performance by categorizing errors into six error types. Experiments with various open and closed MLLMs reveal bottlenecks and factors impeding refinement performance, highlighting areas for improvement in effective reasoning enhancement. Our code and dataset are publicly available at https://github.com/naver-ai/MMRefine.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "MMRefine: Unveiling the Obstacles to Robust Refinement in Multimodal Large Language Models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MMRefine, a novel benchmark specifically designed to evaluate the refinement capabilities of multimodal large language models (MLLMs).  MMRefine goes beyond simply measuring pre- and post-refinement accuracy.  It categorizes refinement outcomes into six distinct scenarios (False Error Detection, Verification Success, Refinement Failure, Error Detection Success, Error Correction Success, Refinement Success) and error types (Problem understanding, Logical Reasoning, Calculation, Equation, Visual perception, and Spatial reasoning), allowing for a more granular analysis of where MLLMs struggle in the refinement process.  The authors conduct experiments on various open-source and closed-source MLLMs, revealing bottlenecks and factors that hinder refinement performance and highlighting areas for improvement in reasoning enhancement.  The code and dataset are publicly available.  The benchmark uses GPT-4o as a judge, with human and OPENAI 01 verification, and reference feedback is generated by OPENAI 01.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper offers significant novelty in its fine-grained analysis of MLLM refinement capabilities.  While previous work has explored self-refinement, MMRefine provides a much more detailed breakdown of the different failure modes and successes, enabling a more targeted approach to improving MLLMs' reasoning abilities. The focus on multimodal reasoning and error analysis is a crucial addition given the rising importance of MLLMs. Categorizing outcomes into six scenarios and errors into six types is a distinct methodological contribution. However, self-correction and iterative refinement has been broadly studied, so the core concept is not ground-breaking.

*   **Significance:** The benchmark addresses a crucial need in the field. As MLLMs become more prevalent in complex tasks, understanding their ability to reliably refine their own outputs is paramount. MMRefine can serve as a valuable tool for researchers to diagnose the weaknesses of existing models and develop more effective refinement strategies. By understanding where models fail in refinement (e.g., difficulty detecting errors in visual perception, issues with spatial reasoning), researchers can create targeted training or architectural improvements. Furthermore, the finding that the models used have different strengths and weaknesses points to the necessity of a more detailed and systematic understanding of MLLM capabilities.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   *Granular Analysis:* The paper's primary strength is its detailed and comprehensive evaluation framework. The six scenarios and error type classifications provide a much richer understanding of MLLM refinement performance than simple accuracy metrics.
    *   *Public Availability:*  Releasing the code and dataset promotes reproducibility and allows other researchers to leverage MMRefine in their own work.
    *   *Comprehensive Evaluation:* Evaluating a diverse set of MLLMs (both open-source and closed-source) provides a broad understanding of the current state of refinement capabilities.
    *   *Analysis of Error Types:* The error type analysis is valuable for identifying specific areas where MLLMs need improvement.
    *   *Use of LLM Judge with Human Verification:*  The methodology for evaluation is sound and addresses a key challenge in evaluating complex reasoning tasks.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   *Reliance on GPT-4o for Evaluation:* While the authors address this by comparing to human annotations, the reliance on a single LLM (GPT-4o) as a judge introduces potential bias and limits the generalizability of the results. The agreement rate with human judgement is not perfect.
    *   *Limited Scope of Problems:* Focusing solely on mathematical problems, while providing a controlled environment, restricts the conclusions that can be drawn about refinement in other domains. While the breadth of math questions is good, expanding the benchmark to other modalities and tasks (e.g., visual question answering with more complex reasoning) would strengthen the conclusions.
    *   *The Reference Solution Limitation:* The adherence to a single reference solution as the *ground truth* can limit the assessment. Since many math problems may have alternative correct methods and solutions, limiting the evaluation on a single one could lead to some bias.
    *   *Computational cost:* Conducting refinement adds to overall computational cost. A study of how efficiently different models refine would add value.
    *   *RefScore:* Although it provides a consolidated metric, the RefScore definition (*RS - FD*) may be too simplistic, potentially masking nuances within the data (e.g., a model could have very high RS *and* FD but still get a mediocre RefScore).
    *   *Effectiveness of VISUALPRM-8B:* The trade-off between error detection and verification success due to VISUALPRM-8B is not well understood. Further exploration and analysis is required to determine the utility of incorporating this method.

*   **Potential Influence:**  MMRefine has the potential to become a standard benchmark for evaluating MLLM refinement. The detailed analysis framework can guide future research in developing more robust and reliable reasoning capabilities.  The benchmark's release could lead to the development of new techniques for improving MLLM performance in diverse domains. The benchmark offers a good framework for dissecting MLLM weaknesses in reasoning, offering a roadmap for improvement.
*   **Other considerations** The initial solutions are taken from a relatively small set of 4 models. It would be more realistic to generate initial solutions from a larger and more diverse pool of MLLMs.

**Justification:**

The MMRefine benchmark provides a significant and useful framework to systematically assess MLLM's refinement abilities and offers a great framework to dissect MLLM's weaknesses in reasoning, which leads to the final score of 7. The weaknesses identified primarily relate to the scope of the benchmark and some methodological choices, but these do not detract from the overall value of the contribution.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Recycling the Web: A Method to Enhance Pre-training Data Quality and Quantity for Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04689v1)
- **Authors**: Thao Nguyen, Yang Li, Olga Golovneva, Luke Zettlemoyer, Sewoong Oh, Ludwig Schmidt, Xian Li
- **Abstract**: Scaling laws predict that the performance of large language models improves with increasing model size and data size. In practice, pre-training has been relying on massive web crawls, using almost all data sources publicly available on the internet so far. However, this pool of natural data does not grow at the same rate as the compute supply. Furthermore, the availability of high-quality texts is even more limited: data filtering pipelines often remove up to 99% of the initial web scrapes to achieve state-of-the-art. To address the "data wall" of pre-training scaling, our work explores ways to transform and recycle data discarded in existing filtering processes. We propose REWIRE, REcycling the Web with guIded REwrite, a method to enrich low-quality documents so that they could become useful for training. This in turn allows us to increase the representation of synthetic data in the final pre-training set. Experiments at 1B, 3B and 7B scales of the DCLM benchmark show that mixing high-quality raw texts and our rewritten texts lead to 1.0, 1.3 and 2.5 percentage points improvement respectively across 22 diverse tasks, compared to training on only filtered web data. Training on the raw-synthetic data mix is also more effective than having access to 2x web data. Through further analysis, we demonstrate that about 82% of the mixed in texts come from transforming lower-quality documents that would otherwise be discarded. REWIRE also outperforms related approaches of generating synthetic data, including Wikipedia-style paraphrasing, question-answer synthesizing and knowledge extraction. These results suggest that recycling web texts holds the potential for being a simple and effective approach for scaling pre-training data.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Recycling the Web: A Method to Enhance Pre-training Data Quality and Quantity for Language Models" introduces REWIRE, a method to improve pre-training data by "recycling" low-quality web documents that are typically discarded by standard filtering processes. REWIRE uses a large language model (LLM) to rewrite these documents, aiming to enrich them and make them suitable for pre-training. The rewritten data is then mixed with high-quality raw data, and the resulting mixture is used to train language models. The authors demonstrate that this approach leads to performance gains on the DCLM benchmark compared to training on only filtered web data or even having access to twice as much raw data. They also compare REWIRE with other synthetic data generation methods, finding that REWIRE produces more diverse and effective training data.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The idea of "recycling" discarded web data is interesting, especially in the context of the "data wall" faced by LLM pre-training. Prompting an LLM for the rewriting is not entirely novel, however, targeting specifically the medium quality data and using the chain of thought reasoning prior the rewriting step is unique. The connection between a medium quality input and the LLM is not obvious and a strong contribution.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial problem in the LLM field: the increasing scarcity of high-quality training data. By demonstrating a method to effectively utilize previously discarded data, the authors offer a promising direction for scaling pre-training datasets. The performance gains on the DCLM benchmark, coupled with the detailed analysis of the rewritten data, provide strong evidence for the effectiveness of REWIRE. Furthermore, the study also shows the potential of reducing the cost of pre-training data by effectively increasing the size of high quality data that is available.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Statement:** The paper clearly articulates the data bottleneck issue in LLM pre-training.
    *   **Well-Defined Method:** REWIRE is a well-defined and easily reproducible method.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The authors conduct extensive experiments across different model sizes and datasets, providing robust evidence for their claims.
    *   **Detailed Analysis:** The paper includes detailed analysis of the rewritten data, including quality metrics, diversity measures, and comparison with other synthetic data generation methods.
    *   **Practical Implications:** The approach has practical implications for LLM development, as it offers a way to reduce the reliance on scarce high-quality data.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Computational Cost:** Rewriting a large dataset is computationally expensive. The use of a large instruct LLM like Llama-3 70B is resource intensive. While the paper states the cost can be amortized by training multiple models, a more detailed cost-benefit analysis would be beneficial.
    *   **Potential for Hallucination:** The paper acknowledges the risk of increased hallucination with synthetic data, although the experimental results do not suggest this to be a serious issue. More rigorous evaluations of factuality would strengthen the claims. The cost and efficiency aspects of REWIRE in comparison to other data augmentation strategies need more discussion.
    *   **Dependency on LLM quality:** The performance benefits depend on the knowledge of the rewriting LLM. This means that LLMs need to be carefully selected to avoid hallucination.

*   **Potential Influence:** REWIRE has the potential to influence future research in LLM pre-training by encouraging the exploration of data recycling strategies. The method's simplicity and effectiveness make it a practical solution for addressing the data bottleneck. Additionally, the comparison with other synthetic data generation techniques can inform future work in this area.

*   **Missing details**: The prompt used in the rewiring step is given in an annex, but not justified at all. This prompt plays a central role in the framework, it is key to understand why the authors have chosen this prompt.

*   **Minor:** The paper refers to DCLM in the name of the method, but doesn't justify why. It's likely that the name can be more general to describe the method properly.

**Justification for Score:**

Despite some limitations, the paper makes a valuable contribution to the LLM field. The REWIRE method addresses a significant problem with a practical and well-evaluated solution. The detailed analysis and comparison with other methods add to the paper's impact. Given the novelty and significance of the approach, I assign it a score of **7**.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Towards Better Generalization via Distributional Input Projection Network
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04690v1)
- **Authors**: Yifan Hao, Yanxin Lu, Xinwei Shen, Tong Zhang
- **Abstract**: As overparameterized models become increasingly prevalent, training loss alone offers limited insight into generalization performance. While smoothness has been linked to improved generalization across various settings, directly enforcing smoothness in neural networks remains challenging. To address this, we introduce Distributional Input Projection Networks (DIPNet), a novel framework that projects inputs into learnable distributions at each layer. This distributional representation induces a smoother loss landscape with respect to the input, promoting better generalization. We provide theoretical analysis showing that DIPNet reduces both local smoothness measures and the Lipschitz constant of the network, contributing to improved generalization performance. Empirically, we validate DIPNet across a wide range of architectures and tasks, including Vision Transformers (ViTs), Large Language Models (LLMs), ResNet and MLPs. Our method consistently enhances test performance under standard settings, adversarial attacks, out-of-distribution inputs, and reasoning benchmarks. We demonstrate that the proposed input projection strategy can be seamlessly integrated into existing models, providing a general and effective approach for boosting generalization performance in modern deep learning.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Distributional Input Projection Networks (DIPNet), a novel architecture designed to improve generalization in deep learning models. DIPNet projects inputs into learnable distributions at each layer, aiming to induce a smoother loss landscape, reduce sensitivity to input variations, and thereby enhance generalization. The authors provide theoretical analysis showing that DIPNet reduces local smoothness measures and Lipschitz constants. Empirically, they demonstrate DIPNet's effectiveness across various architectures (ViTs, LLMs, ResNet, MLPs) and tasks, including standard settings, adversarial attacks, out-of-distribution inputs, and reasoning benchmarks. The paper proposes an efficient training method inspired by variational inference, incorporating a stability-promoting penalty.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of projecting inputs into learnable distributions at each layer is a relatively novel approach to promote smoothness and improve generalization. While existing techniques like adversarial training and random smoothing also aim to improve robustness, DIPNet's architectural approach distinguishes it. Projecting the input to learnable distributions at each layer is novel. However, the broader concept of using stochasticity to improve robustness is related to existing work (e.g., dropout, variational autoencoders).
*   **Significance:** Generalization is a fundamental challenge in deep learning, and the paper tackles this problem by enforcing smoothness in a novel and relatively computationally efficient manner. The method's broad applicability across different architectures and tasks highlights its potential practical significance. The experiments cover a range of important scenarios, including adversarial robustness and out-of-distribution generalization, which are crucial for real-world applications. The LLM results on the mathematical reasoning task are also compelling and suggest that the methods helps generalization for tasks that require more abstract thinking. The theoretical analysis, while not overly complex, provides some justification for the empirical observations.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   The architectural modification is relatively simple and can be integrated into existing models.
    *   The empirical results are comprehensive, demonstrating consistent improvements across various settings.
    *   The theoretical analysis provides some support for the proposed approach.
    *   The paper addresses a well-defined and important problem in deep learning.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The theoretical analysis could be more rigorous and provide tighter bounds.
    *   The method's sensitivity to hyperparameters α and β could be better addressed. While a penalty is included, finding the right weights is critical.
    *   The empirical improvements, while consistent, are sometimes marginal, especially on certain tasks. More substantial gains are seen in adversarial settings.
    *   There may be specific network designs or datasets that DIPNet does not improve, so more work is needed to provide design guidelines.
*   **Justification for Score:** The paper's novelty lies in the architectural integration of distributional projection at each layer, offering a structured stochasticity that promotes smoothness and mitigates sensitivity to input variation. This differs from training-only augmentation. The consistently positive empirical results across diverse tasks, architectures, and challenges like adversarial attacks and distribution shifts support the effectiveness of DIPNet. While the theoretical analysis is not groundbreaking, it does provide theoretical underpinnings for improved generalization. The limitations mostly involve the relatively simple theoretical work and empirical improvements sometimes being small. With the addition of theoretical and numerical work, this paper could be considered a landmark paper in machine learning research.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Cracking the Code: Enhancing Implicit Hate Speech Detection through Coding Classification
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04693v1)
- **Authors**: Lu Wei, Liangzhi Li, Tong Xiang, Xiao Liu, Noa Garcia
- **Abstract**: The internet has become a hotspot for hate speech (HS), threatening societal harmony and individual well-being. While automatic detection methods perform well in identifying explicit hate speech (ex-HS), they struggle with more subtle forms, such as implicit hate speech (im-HS). We tackle this problem by introducing a new taxonomy for im-HS detection, defining six encoding strategies named codetypes. We present two methods for integrating codetypes into im-HS detection: 1) prompting large language models (LLMs) directly to classify sentences based on generated responses, and 2) using LLMs as encoders with codetypes embedded during the encoding process. Experiments show that the use of codetypes improves im-HS detection in both Chinese and English datasets, validating the effectiveness of our approach across different languages.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper tackles the challenge of detecting implicit hate speech (im-HS) on the internet. Unlike explicit hate speech, im-HS uses subtle encoding strategies, making it difficult for standard detection methods to identify.  The authors introduce a novel taxonomy of six "codetypes" (Abbreviation, Metaphor, Irony, Pun, Argot, Idiom) representing rhetorical strategies commonly used in im-HS.  They then present two approaches to incorporating these codetypes into im-HS detection: (1) prompting large language models (LLMs) to classify sentences based on generated responses, and (2) using LLMs as encoders with codetypes embedded during the encoding process.  Experiments on Chinese and English datasets demonstrate that incorporating codetypes improves im-HS detection performance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The most significant novelty lies in the introduction of the "codetype" taxonomy and its application to im-HS detection. While previous work has focused on distinguishing im-HS from ex-HS or explaining why a statement is hateful, this paper directly addresses *how* im-HS is encoded through rhetorical strategies. The codetype taxonomy attempts to provide a more structured way of understanding and detecting implicit hate. The use of LLMs in both prompting and embedding frameworks is not entirely novel, but the specific application with the codetype information is innovative.

*   **Significance:** Improving im-HS detection is important for content moderation and addressing online toxicity. The paper's approach offers a potentially valuable tool for identifying more subtle forms of hate speech that might otherwise evade detection. The fact that the authors tested and validated their approach on both Chinese and English datasets strengthens the claim of cross-linguistic applicability, enhancing the potential significance and wide-ranging utility of the proposed methods.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Codetype Taxonomy:** The key strength is the attempt to categorize and formalize the encoding strategies employed in im-HS. This provides a concrete framework for analysis and detection.
    *   **Cross-Lingual Validation:** Testing the approach on both Chinese and English datasets adds credibility and demonstrates broader applicability.
    *   **LLM Integration:** The paper leverages the capabilities of LLMs in two different ways (prompting and encoding), providing a comprehensive approach.
    *   **Clear Methodology:** The experimental setup is well-described, allowing for potential reproducibility.
    *   **Detailed Analysis of Results:** The paper provides a thoughtful comparison of different methods and codetype combinations, revealing interesting insights into what works best and why.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Codetype Definition and Subjectivity:** Although the authors tried to define codetypes clearly, some codetypes (e.g., irony, metaphor) can still be subjective. Annotator agreement, though moderate, is not very high. Improving the clarity and operationalization of codetype definitions might strengthen the approach.
    *   **Model and Task-Specific:** The results are dependent on the specific LLMs and datasets used. It's unclear how well the approach would generalize to other models or different types of im-HS datasets.
    *   **Limited Evaluation:** While improvements are shown, the F1 scores, especially for certain methods and datasets, are not exceptionally high, indicating that the task remains difficult. More thorough quantitative evaluations would further enhance the validity of the approach.
    *   **Reliance on Wikipedia:** The codetype taxonomy development process uses Wikipedia explanations, which might introduce biases.

*   **Potential Influence:** This paper has the potential to influence future research in im-HS detection by encouraging a more fine-grained analysis of encoding strategies. The codetype taxonomy could serve as a starting point for developing more sophisticated detection methods.
* **Limitations and Future work:** Authors mentioned comprehensiveness of codetype taxonomy is limitation to this work and methods used in this paper. Building a model which dynamiclly adapt different sentence will imporve efficiency of this detection process. Also, for future work, they need to optimize the number of codetypes for statement containing more than 2 codetypes by using chain-of-thought approach to LLM.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel and potentially impactful approach to im-HS detection by introducing the codetype taxonomy. While there are limitations regarding subjectivity, generalizability, and modest F1 scores, the paper's strengths, particularly its cross-lingual validation and detailed analysis, justify a score of 7.  The paper offers a significant contribution to the field and lays the groundwork for future research in this area.

### Empowering Economic Simulation for Massively Multiplayer Online Games through Generative Agent-Based Modeling
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04699v1)
- **Authors**: Bihan Xu, Shiwei Zhao, Runze Wu, Zhenya Huang, Jiawei Wang, Zhipeng Hu, Kai Wang, Haoyu Liu, Tangjie Lv, Le Li, Changjie Fan, Xin Tong, Jiangze Han
- **Abstract**: Within the domain of Massively Multiplayer Online (MMO) economy research, Agent-Based Modeling (ABM) has emerged as a robust tool for analyzing game economics, evolving from rule-based agents to decision-making agents enhanced by reinforcement learning. Nevertheless, existing works encounter significant challenges when attempting to emulate human-like economic activities among agents, particularly regarding agent reliability, sociability, and interpretability. In this study, we take a preliminary step in introducing a novel approach using Large Language Models (LLMs) in MMO economy simulation. Leveraging LLMs' role-playing proficiency, generative capacity, and reasoning aptitude, we design LLM-driven agents with human-like decision-making and adaptability. These agents are equipped with the abilities of role-playing, perception, memory, and reasoning, addressing the aforementioned challenges effectively. Simulation experiments focusing on in-game economic activities demonstrate that LLM-empowered agents can promote emergent phenomena like role specialization and price fluctuations in line with market rules.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores the use of Large Language Models (LLMs) to enhance Agent-Based Modeling (ABM) for simulating economic systems within Massively Multiplayer Online (MMO) games.  Traditional ABM approaches for MMO economics struggle with agent reliability (mimicking diverse player behaviors), sociability (modeling communication and negotiation), and interpretability (understanding agent decision-making). The authors propose an LLM-empowered framework called MMOAgent that leverages the role-playing, generative, and reasoning abilities of LLMs. This framework integrates data-driven agent profile generation (based on real player data), perception (understanding the game environment), reasoning (determining suitable actions), memory (logging game experience), and action (executing actions in the game).  The authors validate their approach through experiments, demonstrating that LLM-empowered agents exhibit more human-like behavior, promote emergent phenomena like role specialization and price fluctuations consistent with market rules, and outperform traditional ABM agents and other baselines. The authors further address limitations within previous MMO economies with P2P(player-to-player) trading using linguistic exchanges such as offers, negotiations, and bargains.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The integration of LLMs into ABM for MMO economic simulation is a valuable, incremental step.  While LLMs have been used in multi-agent simulations before, the paper's focus on a complex, interactive economic environment like an MMO is a significant contribution. It adds valuable features such as P2P trading that previous works were unable to address with human-like trading patterns. The profile generation approach, deriving profiles from real player data and then using LLMs to create textual descriptions, is a good idea for making the agents realistic. This is something that previously used fixed heuristics or reinforcement learning has struggled with.

*   **Significance:**  The ability to simulate MMO economies accurately has both research and practical implications.  For researchers, it provides a powerful tool for studying complex economic dynamics and testing hypotheses about player behavior. For game developers, it can aid in designing balanced and engaging economic systems, predicting the impact of policy changes, and detecting potential exploits. This has practical implications for game balance and the health of the game's economy.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Data-Driven Approach:** Using real player data to generate agent profiles is a key strength, increasing the realism and credibility of the simulation.
    *   **Comprehensive Framework:** The MMOAgent framework is well-designed, incorporating key aspects of human behavior and economic decision-making.
    *   **Empirical Validation:**  The experiments provide convincing evidence that LLM-empowered agents outperform baselines and exhibit realistic economic behavior.
    *   **Addressing Limitations of Traditional ABM:**  The paper clearly identifies and addresses the limitations of rule-based and RL-based ABM, such as the inability to handle complex communication and human-like decision-making.
    *   **P2P Trading:** The paper adds critical aspects of P2P trading using linguistic nuances such as offers, bargaining, and negotiations that were not present in MMO economy predecessors.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **LLM Hallucinations:** The paper acknowledges the issue of LLM "hallucinations" leading to unrealistic actions. While this is a general problem with LLMs, the paper could have explored mitigation strategies further, such as incorporating stronger constraints or feedback mechanisms.
    *   **Computational Cost:** The paper doesn't explicitly address the computational cost of using LLMs for agent behavior. Scaling this approach to simulate large MMO populations might be computationally expensive.
    *   **Limited Generalization:**  The results are specific to the MMO game from which the data was collected. While the framework is modular, its generalizability to other MMOs with different economic structures is not fully demonstrated.
    *   **Reliance on Specific LLMs:** The performance of the framework depends on the capabilities of the underlying LLM. The use of different LLMs (e.g., Llama3) shows some differences in behavior, highlighting this dependency.
    *   **Lack of deeper analysis:** More in-depth exploration into other economic aspects of MMO's would add more significance to the paper.

*   **Impact:** The work has the potential to influence research in both ABM and game economics. It demonstrates the power of LLMs for creating more realistic and intelligent simulations, potentially leading to new approaches for studying complex systems. It's also likely to inspire further research on integrating LLMs with other simulation techniques.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel and well-executed approach to simulating MMO economies using LLMs, addressing important limitations of previous ABM methods. The data-driven agent profiles and comprehensive MMOAgent framework, combined with empirical validation, are strong points. However, the potential for LLM hallucinations, the computational cost, and the reliance on specific LLMs are weaknesses that limit its overall impact. These limitations, along with the lack of generalizability, prevent it from reaching a higher score.

### LLM-based phoneme-to-grapheme for phoneme-based speech recognition
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04711v1)
- **Authors**: Te Ma, Min Bi, Saierdaer Yusuyin, Hao Huang, Zhijian Ou
- **Abstract**: In automatic speech recognition (ASR), phoneme-based multilingual pre-training and crosslingual fine-tuning is attractive for its high data efficiency and competitive results compared to subword-based models. However, Weighted Finite State Transducer (WFST) based decoding is limited by its complex pipeline and inability to leverage large language models (LLMs). Therefore, we propose LLM-based phoneme-to-grapheme (LLM-P2G) decoding for phoneme-based ASR, consisting of speech-to-phoneme (S2P) and phoneme-to-grapheme (P2G). A challenge is that there seems to have information loss in cascading S2P and P2G. To address this challenge, we propose two training strategies: data augmentation with noisy phonemes (DANP), and randomized top-$K$ marginalized (TKM) training and decoding. Our experimental results show that LLM-P2G outperforms WFST-based systems in crosslingual ASR for Polish and German, by relative WER reductions of 3.6% and 6.9% respectively.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LLM-P2G (LLM-based phoneme-to-grapheme) decoding for phoneme-based automatic speech recognition (ASR). This approach aims to replace the traditional WFST-based decoding with a more modern LLM-based approach that can leverage large language models. To address the challenge of information loss in the two-step (speech-to-phoneme and phoneme-to-grapheme) architecture, the authors propose two training strategies: data augmentation with noisy phonemes (DANP) and randomized top-K marginalized (TKM) training. The experiments show that LLM-P2G, especially with the TKM strategy, outperforms WFST-based systems in crosslingual ASR for Polish and German.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**

    *   **Main Idea:** The core idea of using an LLM for phoneme-to-grapheme conversion in a two-step ASR system has some novelty. It builds on previous work in multilingual ASR and leveraging LLMs for various tasks. The novelty lies in *specifically* applying LLMs to the P2G component, acknowledging and attempting to mitigate the information loss inherent in cascading S2P and P2G models.
    *   **Technical Contributions (DANP and TKM):**  The proposed training strategies, DANP and TKM, are a key contribution. DANP is a fairly straightforward data augmentation technique, but TKM and Randomized TKM are more interesting. The idea of marginalizing over top-K phoneme sequences during training and decoding to improve robustness is novel.  The randomized variant enhances this by preventing overfitting to specific S2P rankings.
    *   **Scope Limitation:** The scope is somewhat limited. It focuses mainly on crosslingual ASR with specific languages (Polish, German), and uses a specific LLM (mT5-base) and S2P backbone (Whistle). While this allows for controlled experiments and comparison, the generalizability of the findings to other languages, LLMs, or acoustic models needs further investigation.

*   **Significance:**

    *   **Practical Impact:** The work has potential practical impact by offering a simpler and more efficient decoding pipeline for phoneme-based ASR, potentially enabling better leveraging of LLMs in low-resource scenarios. The reported WER reductions for Polish and German are significant.
    *   **Theoretical Advance:** The paper contributes to the understanding of how to effectively integrate LLMs into two-step ASR architectures and how to mitigate information loss in cascaded models. It highlights the importance of aligning training and decoding strategies when using LLMs for P2G.
    *   **Community Influence:** The release of code, models, and data will contribute to the research community by allowing others to reproduce, validate, and extend this work. The comparison to a solid baseline (Whistle with WFST) also strengthens the evaluation.
    *   **Dependency on Existing Systems** LLM-P2G's performance is very reliant on Whistle-S2P for generating phonemes, inheriting any biases or weaknesses from Whistle.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Statement:** The paper clearly articulates the limitations of WFST-based decoding and the potential benefits of LLM-based approaches.
    *   **Well-Defined Methods:**  The proposed methods (DANP and TKM) are well-defined and relatively easy to understand.
    *   **Comprehensive Experiments:** The experiments are comprehensive, with ablation studies that examine the impact of different training strategies and hyper-parameters.
    *   **Significant Performance Improvements:**  The results demonstrate significant improvements in WER, especially with the randomized TKM strategy.
    *   **Reproducibility:** The availability of code, models and data is a huge boon for the scientific community.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope:**  The focus on only two languages and a specific LLM limits the generalizability of the findings.
    *   **Incremental Novelty:** While the combination of LLM and specific training methods is novel, the individual components build upon existing techniques.
    *   **Lack of Qualitative Analysis:** The paper lacks qualitative analysis, such as error analysis or examples of how LLM-P2G corrects errors made by the S2P model. This would provide deeper insights into the benefits of the approach.
    *   **Dependency on Data:** A large caveat of the P2G approach is it's dependent on training and fine-tuning, and may perform poorly if there are data limitations.
    *   **Inadequate LM Comparison:** It might have been informative to analyze LLM-P2G's intrinsic language modeling capacity by evaluating it directly on perplexity, particularly in comparison to the n-gram language model used in the WFST baseline.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel approach to phoneme-based ASR by leveraging LLMs for the phoneme-to-grapheme conversion. The proposed training strategies (DANP and, especially, randomized TKM) are significant contributions, mitigating the information loss issue in cascaded S2P-P2G systems. The performance improvements, particularly in crosslingual scenarios, are convincing. However, the limited scope of the experiments (two languages, specific LLM), the incremental nature of the novelty (building on existing techniques), and the lack of deeper qualitative analysis prevent a higher score. The dependency on the Whistle-S2P system for initial phoneme generation is another limitation.

### Towards Holistic Visual Quality Assessment of AI-Generated Videos: A LLM-Based Multi-Dimensional Evaluation Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04715v1)
- **Authors**: Zelu Qi, Ping Shi, Chaoyang Zhang, Shuqi Wang, Fei Zhao, Da Pan, Zefeng Ying
- **Abstract**: The development of AI-Generated Video (AIGV) technology has been remarkable in recent years, significantly transforming the paradigm of video content production. However, AIGVs still suffer from noticeable visual quality defects, such as noise, blurriness, frame jitter and low dynamic degree, which severely impact the user's viewing experience. Therefore, an effective automatic visual quality assessment is of great importance for AIGV content regulation and generative model improvement. In this work, we decompose the visual quality of AIGVs into three dimensions: technical quality, motion quality, and video semantics. For each dimension, we design corresponding encoder to achieve effective feature representation. Moreover, considering the outstanding performance of large language models (LLMs) in various vision and language tasks, we introduce a LLM as the quality regression module. To better enable the LLM to establish reasoning associations between multi-dimensional features and visual quality, we propose a specially designed multi-modal prompt engineering framework. Additionally, we incorporate LoRA fine-tuning technology during the training phase, allowing the LLM to better adapt to specific tasks. Our proposed method achieved \textbf{second place} in the NTIRE 2025 Quality Assessment of AI-Generated Content Challenge: Track 2 AI Generated video, demonstrating its effectiveness. Codes can be obtained at https://github.com/QiZelu/AIGVEval.
- **Summary**: Okay, I'll provide a summary and critical evaluation of the paper based on the provided text.

**Summary:**

The paper "Towards Holistic Visual Quality Assessment of AI-Generated Videos: A LLM-Based Multi-Dimensional Evaluation Model" proposes a new approach (AIGVEval) for automatically assessing the visual quality of AI-Generated Videos (AIGVs). The approach decomposes the video quality into three dimensions: technical quality (blurriness, noise), motion quality (dynamic degree, frame jitter), and video semantics (color, texture, scene description).  For each dimension, a dedicated encoder (BLIP, 3D Swin Transformer, SlowFast-R50) extracts features.  A Large Language Model (LLM) (Vicuna v1.5 7B) then acts as the quality regression module. To enhance LLM reasoning, the authors designed a multi-modal prompt engineering framework utilizing semantic anchors. Additionally, they incorporate LoRA fine-tuning to adapt the LLM to the specific AIGV quality assessment task. The authors achieved second place in the NTIRE 2025 challenge demonstrating the effectiveness of their approach.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Multi-Dimensional Approach:** Decomposing video quality into distinct dimensions (technical, motion, semantics) is a logical and potentially more effective strategy than a single monolithic approach. This allows for targeted feature extraction and potentially more interpretable quality assessments.
    *   **Use of LLM and Prompt Engineering:** Leveraging LLMs for video quality assessment is a promising direction. The prompt engineering with semantic anchors is a key contribution. It attempts to bridge the gap between low-level visual features and high-level semantic understanding for more nuanced quality evaluation. The semantic anchors helps the LLM in better understanding the encoder's multi-dimensional features.
    *   **LoRA Fine-tuning:** Incorporating LoRA for efficient adaptation of the LLM to the AIGV task is a practical and important design choice, considering the computational cost of full LLM fine-tuning.
    *   **NTIRE Challenge Result:** Achieving second place in the NTIRE 2025 challenge provides empirical evidence of the effectiveness of the proposed method in a real-world setting.
    *   **Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contribution of each component (technical, motion, and semantic encoders, semantic anchors). They demonstrate that all components contribute positively to the overall performance.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Dependency on Pre-trained Models:** The reliance on pre-trained models (BLIP, 3D Swin Transformer, SlowFast-R50) means that the performance is limited by the capabilities of these models and the datasets they were trained on. The approach might not generalize well to AIGVs with characteristics significantly different from those seen during the pre-training of those encoders.
    *   **Limited Novelty in Basic Architecture:** The overall encoder-decoder architecture with pre-trained encoders and a regression head (LLM) is relatively standard. The main novelty lies in the specific combination of encoders, the prompt engineering, and the fine-tuning strategy.
    *   **Lack of Comparison to Strong Baselines:** While the paper compares with several other methods in the NTIRE competition, it does not include some of the more recent VQA models.
    *   **Dataset Specificity:** The Q-Eval dataset is used, but the authors also mention the inconsistency with text-video alignment when compared to T2VQA-DB, which indicates that the method is sensitive to the nature of the dataset and potential weaknesses when considering alignment.

*   **Significance:** The paper makes a contribution by demonstrating the effectiveness of LLM-based approaches for AIGV quality assessment. The proposed method is one of the better ones to-date.

**Justification of Score:**

I'm assigning a score of **7**.

*   The paper presents a valuable and practical approach for a relevant problem: evaluating the quality of AI-generated videos.
*   The use of an LLM with a well-designed prompt engineering framework and LoRA fine-tuning is a strength.
*   The multi-dimensional decomposition of video quality is also a positive aspect.
*   The NTIRE challenge results provide evidence of the method's effectiveness.
*   However, the architectural novelty is limited, and the reliance on pre-trained models and potential dataset sensitivity are weaknesses.

Therefore, while the paper is a solid contribution with empirical validation, it doesn't represent a breakthrough or fundamentally new paradigm shift that would justify a higher score. The semantic anchor prompt and comprehensive evaluation across encoders makes the paper a strong contribution.

### Learning dissection trajectories from expert surgical videos via imitation learning with equivariant diffusion
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04716v1)
- **Authors**: Hongyu Wang, Yonghao Long, Yueyao Chen, Hon-Chi Yip, Markus Scheppach, Philip Wai-Yan Chiu, Yeung Yam, Helen Mei-Ling Meng, Qi Dou
- **Abstract**: Endoscopic Submucosal Dissection (ESD) is a well-established technique for removing epithelial lesions. Predicting dissection trajectories in ESD videos offers significant potential for enhancing surgical skill training and simplifying the learning process, yet this area remains underexplored. While imitation learning has shown promise in acquiring skills from expert demonstrations, challenges persist in handling uncertain future movements, learning geometric symmetries, and generalizing to diverse surgical scenarios. To address these, we introduce a novel approach: Implicit Diffusion Policy with Equivariant Representations for Imitation Learning (iDPOE). Our method models expert behavior through a joint state action distribution, capturing the stochastic nature of dissection trajectories and enabling robust visual representation learning across various endoscopic views. By incorporating a diffusion model into policy learning, iDPOE ensures efficient training and sampling, leading to more accurate predictions and better generalization. Additionally, we enhance the model's ability to generalize to geometric symmetries by embedding equivariance into the learning process. To address state mismatches, we develop a forward-process guided action inference strategy for conditional sampling. Using an ESD video dataset of nearly 2000 clips, experimental results show that our approach surpasses state-of-the-art methods, both explicit and implicit, in trajectory prediction. To the best of our knowledge, this is the first application of imitation learning to surgical skill development for dissection trajectory prediction.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel approach called Implicit Diffusion Policy with Equivariant Representations for Imitation Learning (iDPOE) to predict dissection trajectories in endoscopic submucosal dissection (ESD) videos.  It tackles the challenges of uncertainty in future movements, geometric symmetries, and generalization to diverse surgical scenarios, all common in imitation learning for surgical skills.  iDPOE uses a diffusion model to implicitly model expert behaviors via a joint state-action distribution, incorporates equivariant representations to handle geometric symmetries (rotations), and employs a forward-process guided action inference strategy to ensure consistent state-action sampling.  The method is evaluated on a custom ESD video dataset, demonstrating superior performance compared to existing trajectory prediction techniques. The paper emphasizes efficient training, improved accuracy, and better generalization capabilities.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in the combination of several ideas within the context of dissection trajectory prediction. The application of diffusion models to implicitly learn surgical policies is a significant step. Specifically, fusing implicit modeling with diffusion models to tackle surgical trajectories has not been explored much. The explicit integration of equivariant representations to address rotational symmetry is a valuable addition. However, each of these components has seen prior use in other domains. The integration of these ideas, specifically for endoscopic surgery, offers a novel contribution, but the individual components are not fundamentally new. Prior work exists on imitation learning, diffusion models, and equivariant representations individually, so the originality is in the *specific combination and adaptation*.

*   **Significance:** Trajectory prediction in surgical procedures is a significant and practically relevant problem. If successful, such systems can aid in surgical training, reduce errors, and improve patient outcomes. The paper addresses a real-world problem with a potentially high impact. Showing superior performance on a challenging and clinically relevant dataset further boosts the significance. The clinical adoption of similar models will depend on rigorous trials; however, such models hold great value for both novice surgeons learning the surgical process or for providing feedback.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Comprehensive evaluation on a custom, real-world surgical dataset.
    *   Clear problem formulation and well-defined method.
    *   Ablation studies isolating the contribution of each component (implicit modeling, equivariance, guidance).
    *   Addresses a practically relevant problem with potential clinical impact.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   While the combination is novel, the individual components (diffusion models, equivariance) are relatively well-established.
    *   The paper could benefit from a deeper discussion of limitations, such as the reliance on high-quality expert demonstrations, and the potential challenges in applying the model to different surgical settings (other types of surgery).
    *   Computational complexity, although discussed, could be a barrier to real-time application and practical deployment. Quantifying this more deeply will be valuable, but also strategies to circumvent the cost of this model.

*   **Justification:**  The paper represents a solid contribution to the field by successfully applying and integrating existing techniques (diffusion models, equivariance) to a practical and important problem (surgical trajectory prediction). The empirical results are convincing and the ablation studies provide useful insights. The relatively incremental nature of the core technical contributions compared to the larger literature on diffusion models and equivariant learning prevents a higher score.

### Lifelong Evolution: Collaborative Learning between Large and Small Language Models for Continuous Emergent Fake News Detection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04739v1)
- **Authors**: Ziyi Zhou, Xiaoming Zhang, Litian Zhang, Yibo Zhang, Zhenyu Guan, Chaozhuo Li, Philip S. Yu
- **Abstract**: The widespread dissemination of fake news on social media has significantly impacted society, resulting in serious consequences. Conventional deep learning methodologies employing small language models (SLMs) suffer from extensive supervised training requirements and difficulties adapting to evolving news environments due to data scarcity and distribution shifts. Large language models (LLMs), despite robust zero-shot capabilities, fall short in accurately detecting fake news owing to outdated knowledge and the absence of suitable demonstrations. In this paper, we propose a novel Continuous Collaborative Emergent Fake News Detection (C$^2$EFND) framework to address these challenges. The C$^2$EFND framework strategically leverages both LLMs' generalization power and SLMs' classification expertise via a multi-round collaborative learning framework. We further introduce a lifelong knowledge editing module based on a Mixture-of-Experts architecture to incrementally update LLMs and a replay-based continue learning method to ensure SLMs retain prior knowledge without retraining entirely. Extensive experiments on Pheme and Twitter16 datasets demonstrate that C$^2$EFND significantly outperforms existed methods, effectively improving detection accuracy and adaptability in continuous emergent fake news scenarios.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces C2EFND (Continuous Collaborative Emergent Fake News Detection), a novel framework that leverages both large language models (LLMs) and small language models (SLMs) for continuous detection of emergent fake news. C2EFND uses a multi-round collaborative learning framework, a lifelong knowledge editing module for LLMs based on Mixture-of-Experts, and a replay-based continue learning method for SLMs. The goal is to address the challenges of data scarcity, continuous knowledge updating, and effective collaboration between LLMs and SLMs in the dynamic context of fake news on social media. Experimental results on Pheme and Twitter16 datasets are presented to demonstrate the effectiveness of C2EFND compared to existing methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper presents a reasonably novel approach to continuous fake news detection. While previous work has explored using LLMs and SLMs individually or in conjunction, the specific combination of techniques in C2EFND (MoE-based lifelong knowledge editing for LLMs, replay-based continual learning for SLMs, a two-stage active learning approach and a multi-round iterative learning framework) distinguishes it. The idea of incrementally updating an LLM to maintain awareness of emergent news events is particularly interesting.
* **Significance:** The significance of the paper lies in addressing the practical challenges of detecting fake news in a constantly evolving information landscape. The approach attempts to tackle the critical problem of how to continuously update fake news detection models with minimal labeled data, while also preventing catastrophic forgetting. Furthermore, its ability to maintain consistent adaptability to evolving events is a significant contribution that addresses the limitations of existing models. The results on the Pheme and Twitter16 datasets support the claim that C2EFND outperforms existing methods, suggesting it provides a better and more realistic approach to fake news detection.
* **Strengths:**
    * **Comprehensive Framework:** C2EFND provides a holistic framework encompassing data acquisition, model updating, and collaborative inference.
    * **Continual Learning Focus:** The explicit focus on continual learning, specifically addressing catastrophic forgetting, is a major strength.
    * **Hybrid Approach:** The effective synergy between LLMs and SLMs leverages the strengths of both types of models.
    * **Solid Experimental Results:** The experiments demonstrate C2EFND's superiority over various baseline methods on two real-world datasets.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Complexity:** The framework is quite complex, involving multiple components and hyperparameters, which could make it difficult to implement and tune in practice.  The parameter sensitivity analysis, while present, could be more extensive.
    * **Dependency on LLM as a Teacher:** The reliance on the LLM to generate rationales and for knowledge extraction introduces a potential bias. Any inherent limitations or biases in the LLM will be propagated to the SLM. How the impact is mitigated by the iterative selection is not fully explicit.
    * **Limited Analysis of Edge Cases:** Although the quantitative results are promising, the paper could benefit from more qualitative analysis, including examples where C2EFND succeeds or fails. This would provide deeper insights into the framework's strengths and limitations.
    * **No comparison to more recent LLM-based fake news detection techniques:**  While it compares to EFND and ARG, both published a few years ago, there isn't comparison to more recent papers using similar methodology.

* **Potential Impact:** If validated and refined further, C2EFND could have a substantial impact on how fake news detection systems are designed and deployed. The concept of continuous knowledge updating and collaborative learning is applicable to various other domains where models must adapt to changing data distributions.

**Justification of Score:**

While the paper demonstrates a good contribution to the field, it has several limitations that prevent it from achieving a higher score. The novelty is reasonable, but not groundbreaking, as it combines existing techniques in a new way.  The experiments provide adequate evidence to support the core claims, but there could be additional robustness tests and comparison with more recent works. The lack of edge-case analysis limits understanding of how and when it will or will not work in practice. Additionally, the method's complexity might hinder widespread adoption.
