### Multi-Layer GRPO: Enhancing Reasoning and Self-Correction in Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04746v1)
- **Authors**: Fei Ding, Baiqiao Wang, Zijian Zeng, Youwei Wang
- **Abstract**: The Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) algorithm has demonstrated considerable success in enhancing the reasoning capabilities of large language models (LLMs), as evidenced by DeepSeek-R1. However, the absence of intermediate supervision in GRPO frequently leads to inefficient exploration dynamics. A single error in a complex reasoning chain can invalidate the entire solution, resulting in abrupt reward vanishing and compromising training stability.To address these challenges, we propose MGRPO (Multi-layer GRPO). MGRPO operates in two layers: the first layer employs standard GRPO to generate an initial response. This response, along with the original query, is then fed into a second-layer GRPO process. This second layer is specifically trained to identify and correct errors in the initial response, effectively creating a self-correction loop. This mechanism provides implicit process-level supervision by rewarding successful error correction, without requiring an explicit, densely-annotated reward model. Experimental results on several mathematical reasoning benchmarks demonstrate that MGRPO significantly outperforms standard GRPO, achieving superior performance by fostering both reasoning and self-correction abilities.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Multi-Layer GRPO: Enhancing Reasoning and Self-Correction in Large Language Models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Multi-Layer Group Relative Policy Optimization (MGRPO), an extension of the GRPO algorithm designed to improve reasoning and self-correction capabilities in large language models (LLMs).  MGRPO uses a two-layer GRPO process. The first layer generates an initial response using standard GRPO. The second layer refines the initial response, specifically trained to identify and correct errors. This creates a self-correction loop and provides implicit process-level supervision. The authors demonstrate through experiments on mathematical reasoning benchmarks that MGRPO outperforms standard GRPO, achieving higher performance by enhancing both reasoning and self-correction abilities.  The key idea is to leverage the model's own output as a basis for critical review and correction, encouraging learning from errors, similar to dense process rewards but without needing a separate reward model.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The concept of using the model's own output to refine and correct itself isn't entirely novel (self-correction fine-tuning and other iterative refinement approaches have been explored). However, the specific implementation of this concept within the GRPO framework, creating a two-layer GRPO structure where the second layer is dedicated to correction and explicitly trained for this purpose, is relatively novel.  The "correction-augmentation-selection" mechanism adds to this novelty by focusing training on successful corrections.  The application to GRPO, particularly to address the limitations of sparse outcome rewards, is a valuable contribution.

**Significance:**  The paper's significance lies in addressing a practical challenge in applying reinforcement learning to LLMs – the difficulty of dense reward design. By creating a self-correction loop with implicit process supervision, MGRPO offers a way to improve reasoning and self-correction without needing explicitly engineered dense rewards or training separate reward models.  The empirical results show significant improvements over standard GRPO across multiple challenging mathematical reasoning benchmarks, demonstrating the method's effectiveness. This is particularly relevant as real-world applications often rely on rule-based or sparse reward systems. The results on standard datasets like MATH, GSM8K, and OlympiadBench make the contribution accessible and comparable. The focus on reducing resource consumption is also practically significant. The paper presents a valuable, implementable technique to enhance LLM reasoning, reducing reliance on expensive, hand-crafted dense rewards.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the limitations of sparse outcome rewards in GRPO and motivates the need for process supervision.
*   **Well-Defined Approach:** MGRPO is clearly explained with its two-layer structure and correction-augmentation-selection mechanism.
*   **Strong Empirical Results:** The experimental results consistently demonstrate the superiority of MGRPO over standard GRPO and other baselines across several datasets. The use of metrics designed to tease out the contributions of correction is also a strength.
*   **Practical Relevance:** MGRPO addresses a practical challenge in RL for LLMs (reward design) and provides a method that improves performance without needing separate reward models.
*   **Accessible Implementation:** GRPO is a well-documented algorithm and this work builds on that making it more accessible.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Ablation Studies:** While the paper shows MGRPO is better than standard GRPO, it lacks a deeper ablation study.  For example, how important is the correction-augmentation-selection mechanism?  How does the performance vary with different types of guiding phrases (Pguide)? More in-depth analysis on the type of errors it can correct could further increase confidence.
*   **Reliance on Rule-Based Verifier:**  The method still relies on a rule-based verifier for evaluation, which limits its applicability to tasks where such verifiers are available. Addressing how this method can work with other sparse rewards would improve the significance of this paper.
*   **Incremental Improvement:** While the performance gains are significant, they might be seen as incremental improvements on existing methods. The core idea of self-correction is not entirely novel, and MGRPO builds upon existing techniques.

**Justification for Score:**

MGRPO presents a solid and practical contribution to improving reasoning and self-correction in LLMs using reinforcement learning. The improvements over standard GRPO are significant and the method provides a way to improve process supervision without requiring complex dense reward engineering. Although the core idea of self-correction is not entirely new, the specific implementation within the GRPO framework, the use of the correction-augmentation-selection mechanism, and strong empirical validation make this a valuable contribution.  However, the lack of more detailed ablation studies and the reliance on rule-based verifiers limit the overall impact and wider adoption. Given these factors, I think a score of 7 is justified. This indicates a good research contribution with tangible benefits.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Truth in the Few: High-Value Data Selection for Efficient Multi-Modal Reasoning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04755v1)
- **Authors**: Shenshen Li, Kaiyuan Deng, Lei Wang, Hao Yang, Chong Peng, Peng Yan, Fumin Shen, Heng Tao Shen, Xing Xu
- **Abstract**: While multi-modal large language models (MLLMs) have made significant progress in complex reasoning tasks via reinforcement learning, it is commonly believed that extensive training data is necessary for improving multi-modal reasoning ability, inevitably leading to data redundancy and substantial computational costs. However, can smaller high-value datasets match or outperform full corpora for multi-modal reasoning in MLLMs? In this work, we challenge this assumption through a key observation: meaningful multi-modal reasoning is triggered by only a sparse subset of training samples, termed cognitive samples, whereas the majority contribute marginally. Building on this insight, we propose a novel data selection paradigm termed Reasoning Activation Potential (RAP), which identifies cognitive samples by estimating each sample's potential to stimulate genuine multi-modal reasoning by two complementary estimators: 1) Causal Discrepancy Estimator (CDE) based on the potential outcome model principle, eliminates samples that overly rely on language priors by comparing outputs between multi-modal and text-only inputs; 2) Attention Confidence Estimator (ACE), which exploits token-level self-attention to discard samples dominated by irrelevant but over-emphasized tokens in intermediate reasoning stages. Moreover, we introduce a Difficulty-aware Replacement Module (DRM) to substitute trivial instances with cognitively challenging ones, thereby ensuring complexity for robust multi-modal reasoning. Experiments on six datasets show that our RAP method consistently achieves superior performance using only 9.3% of the training data, while reducing computational costs by over 43%. Our code is available at https://github.com/Leo-ssl/RAP.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Truth in the Few: High-Value Data Selection for Efficient Multi-Modal Reasoning."

**Summary**

The paper addresses the problem of data redundancy and high computational costs associated with training multi-modal large language models (MLLMs) for complex reasoning tasks. It challenges the common belief that extensive training data is necessary and proposes a novel data selection paradigm called Reasoning Activation Potential (RAP). RAP aims to identify a small subset of "cognitive samples" that effectively trigger multi-modal reasoning. It achieves this through two complementary estimators: (1) Causal Discrepancy Estimator (CDE), which eliminates samples that overly rely on language priors by comparing outputs of multi-modal and text-only inputs; and (2) Attention Confidence Estimator (ACE), which discards samples dominated by irrelevant tokens using token-level self-attention. Furthermore, the paper introduces a Difficulty-aware Replacement Module (DRM) to substitute trivial samples with more cognitively challenging ones. Experiments on several datasets demonstrate that RAP achieves superior performance using only a small fraction (around 9%) of the full training data and reduces computational costs significantly.

**Critical Evaluation**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Novelty of the Approach:** The central idea of identifying "cognitive samples" for efficient MLLM training is a valuable contribution. RAP provides a tangible framework to accomplish this. The combination of CDE and ACE offers a multi-faceted approach to data selection, considering both output-level discrepancies and process-level attention. The DRM also adds another layer to the paradigm by enriching the sample pool with challenging cognitive samples.
    *   **Clear Problem Statement and Motivation:** The paper clearly articulates the problem of data redundancy in MLLM training and motivates the need for high-value data selection. The observations regarding language-prior bias and attention-bias are insightful and well-supported.
    *   **Solid Experimental Results:** The experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of RAP across multiple datasets and with different base models. The results consistently show superior performance compared to using the full dataset and previous data selection methods. The reduction in training time is also a significant advantage.
    *   **Thorough Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contributions of each component of RAP (CDE, ACE, DRM). These studies help justify the design choices and demonstrate the importance of each module.
    *   **Insightful Qualitative Analysis:** The visualization of cognitive samples and the comparison of reasoning processes with LIMR provide a deeper understanding of how RAP improves multi-modal reasoning.
    *   **Generalizability:** The findings that RAP works well across different base models like Qwen2.5-VL-3b/7b and InternVL3-2b, and the validation of RAP in the setting of different reinforcement learning algorithms (e.g., RLOO) suggest the wide applicability of the RAP.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:** While the paper discusses hyperparameter sensitivity, the process of selecting the optimal values for λε and λα could be further elaborated. A more systematic approach to hyperparameter tuning might be beneficial.
    *   **Computational Overhead of RAP components:** While the data selection is fast enough to justify its usage (as suggested in the paper), the individual computational cost incurred by the CDE and ACE components and the DRM could be more explicitly quantified in the paper.
    *   **Reliance on GRPO:** The paper relies on GRPO as the RL method for the training phase. While it is a well-established method, it could be helpful to show if other reinforcement learning paradigms (e.g., directly fine-tuning the LLM using human feedback) could still leverage the benefit of RAP.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The paper addresses a critical challenge in the field of MLLMs: the need for efficient training methods. RAP offers a promising solution that could significantly reduce computational costs and data redundancy.
    *   The concept of "cognitive samples" and the proposed data selection paradigm could influence future research in MLLM training and data curation.
    *   The paper could potentially lead to the development of more efficient and robust MLLMs for a wide range of applications.

**Overall Assessment:**

The paper presents a novel and well-executed approach to efficient MLLM training. The concept of identifying "cognitive samples" is a significant contribution, and the RAP framework provides a concrete way to achieve this. The experimental results and ablation studies provide strong evidence for the effectiveness of the proposed method. Despite the minor weaknesses mentioned above, the paper has the potential to have a significant impact on the field of MLLMs.

Score: 8

**Rationale:** The paper makes a very substantial and valuable contribution, and is extremely well designed. While it's important to acknowledge potential minor limitations, the significance of the paper in terms of promoting resource efficiency for MLLM research, along with its insights into the nature of multi-modal learning, strongly justifies this score. The work is carefully validated with solid experimental designs.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Exp4Fuse: A Rank Fusion Framework for Enhanced Sparse Retrieval using Large Language Model-based Query Expansion
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04760v1)
- **Authors**: Lingyuan Liu, Mengxiang Zhang
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have shown potential in generating hypothetical documents for query expansion, thereby enhancing information retrieval performance. However, the efficacy of this method is highly dependent on the quality of the generated documents, which often requires complex prompt strategies and the integration of advanced dense retrieval techniques. This can be both costly and computationally intensive. To mitigate these limitations, we explore the use of zero-shot LLM-based query expansion to improve sparse retrieval, particularly for learned sparse retrievers. We introduce a novel fusion ranking framework, Exp4Fuse, which enhances the performance of sparse retrievers through an indirect application of zero-shot LLM-based query expansion. Exp4Fuse operates by simultaneously considering two retrieval routes-one based on the original query and the other on the LLM-augmented query. It then generates two ranked lists using a sparse retriever and fuses them using a modified reciprocal rank fusion method. We conduct extensive evaluations of Exp4Fuse against leading LLM-based query expansion methods and advanced retrieval techniques on three MS MARCO-related datasets and seven low-resource datasets. Experimental results reveal that Exp4Fuse not only surpasses existing LLM-based query expansion methods in enhancing sparse retrievers but also, when combined with advanced sparse retrievers, achieves SOTA results on several benchmarks. This highlights the superior performance and effectiveness of Exp4Fuse in improving query expansion for sparse retrieval.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Exp4Fuse, a novel rank fusion framework designed to enhance sparse retrieval by leveraging large language model (LLM)-based query expansion. Unlike conventional LLM-based query expansion methods that often require complex prompting and integration with dense retrieval techniques, Exp4Fuse operates by indirectly applying zero-shot LLM-based query expansion. It simultaneously considers two retrieval routes: one using the original query and another using an LLM-augmented query. The resulting ranked lists from these routes are then fused using a modified reciprocal rank fusion method. The authors evaluate Exp4Fuse extensively against existing LLM-based query expansion techniques and advanced retrieval methods across various datasets, demonstrating its superior performance in enhancing sparse retrievers and achieving state-of-the-art (SOTA) results when combined with advanced sparse retrievers.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The key novelty lies in the rank fusion approach *itself*. The idea of using an LLM in a zero-shot manner for query expansion isn't entirely new (HyDE, query2doc), but the specific way Exp4Fuse *fuses* the results of a standard retrieval and LLM-expanded retrieval using a modified reciprocal rank fusion is. This indirect application of LLMs to sparse retrieval and the modified fusion method is a fresh take. The core idea is using a single lightweight sparse retriever instead of heavier dense retrievers.

*   **Significance:** The significance is multi-fold:

    *   **Practicality:** Exp4Fuse aims to address the limitations of direct LLM-based query expansion, particularly its computational cost and dependency on high-quality generated documents. By fusing results, it reduces reliance on *perfect* LLM-generated expansions.
    *   **Performance:** The paper shows that Exp4Fuse consistently improves sparse retrieval, including learned sparse retrievers. Furthermore, combining with advanced sparse retrievers they achieve SOTA results on several benchmarks.
    *   **Cost-effectiveness:** Claims of the method being cost-effective. Because only a single sparse retriever is used, the resources consumed are significantly lower when compared to having multiple retrievers.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Thorough Evaluation:** The paper presents a comprehensive evaluation with a variety of datasets (MS MARCO, TREC DL, BEIR), demonstrating the robustness of the approach. They directly compare against strong baselines.
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper effectively outlines the limitations of existing LLM-based query expansion methods for sparse retrieval.
    *   **SOTA Results:** Exp4Fuse achieves state-of-the-art results on several benchmarks.
    *   **Reproducibility:** The code is made publicly available and the paper provides sufficient details for potential reproducibility.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dependency on LLM Quality:** While indirect, Exp4Fuse still depends on the quality of the LLM-generated hypothetical documents. A poor LLM could negatively impact the results despite the fusion mechanism.
    *   **Fusion Parameter Tuning:** The performance of Exp4Fuse depends on the weight `λ` given to the original query when combining it with the hypothetical documents. The authors specify that X = 5 is used, but don't mention how this value was derived, only citing that it was derived from a pilot study.
    *   **Limited Ablation Study:** The ablation study lacks rigor. The number of route retrievals are analyzed, but the contributions of individual factors aren't clearly analyzed.

*   **Potential Influence:** Exp4Fuse offers a practical and effective method for improving sparse retrieval by incorporating LLM-based knowledge, which is valuable for many applications. The method can lead to the development of more computationally efficient and easily deployable information retrieval systems. Furthermore, the proposed architecture opens up avenues for future exploration in retrieval, especially in finding the right balance between dense and sparse models.

*   **Critical Notes:**
    * There is reliance on GPT-4, which may not be available to everyone. The authors do use GPT4-mini as the backbone.
    * The modified reciprocal rank fusion method is only a slight modification to existing rank fusion methods. The only modification to existing methods is incorporation of an adaptive weight strategy.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:** Exp4Fuse presents a practical and empirically effective approach to enhancing sparse retrieval using LLMs. Its main strengths are its thorough evaluation, achieved SOTA results on some benchmarks, and cost-effectiveness due to the use of lightweight resources. The novelty of the proposed architecture does exist, and the significance will be amplified once its been proven that lightweight resources are sufficient for high performance retrieval. However, it still depends on LLM quality to some extent, relies on parameter tuning, and has a limited ablation study which lowers the score. Overall, it's a solid contribution that advances the state of the art.
- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Log-Linear Attention
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04761v1)
- **Authors**: Han Guo, Songlin Yang, Tarushii Goel, Eric P. Xing, Tri Dao, Yoon Kim
- **Abstract**: The attention mechanism in Transformers is an important primitive for accurate and scalable sequence modeling. Its quadratic-compute and linear-memory complexity however remain significant bottlenecks. Linear attention and state-space models enable linear-time, constant-memory sequence modeling and can moreover be trained efficiently through matmul-rich parallelization across sequence length. However, at their core these models are still RNNs, and thus their use of a fixed-size hidden state to model the context is a fundamental limitation. This paper develops log-linear attention, an attention mechanism that balances linear attention's efficiency and the expressiveness of softmax attention. Log-linear attention replaces the fixed-size hidden state with a logarithmically growing set of hidden states. We show that with a particular growth function, log-linear attention admits a similarly matmul-rich parallel form whose compute cost is log-linear in sequence length. Log-linear attention is a general framework and can be applied on top of existing linear attention variants. As case studies, we instantiate log-linear variants of two recent architectures -- Mamba-2 and Gated DeltaNet -- and find they perform well compared to their linear-time variants.
- **Summary**: This paper introduces "log-linear attention," an attention mechanism that aims to bridge the gap between the efficiency of linear attention and the expressiveness of softmax attention. The key idea is to replace the fixed-size hidden state of linear attention with a logarithmically growing set of hidden states, managed using a Fenwick tree structure. This allows the model to attend to a logarithmic number of hidden states capturing context at multiple temporal scales. The authors show that with a specific growth function, log-linear attention can be parallelized for training using a chunking mechanism, resulting in a compute cost that is log-linear in sequence length and memory cost that is logarithmic. They demonstrate the effectiveness of log-linear attention by applying it to Mamba-2 and Gated DeltaNet, showing improved performance compared to their linear-time counterparts in some experiments.

**Novelty and Significance:**

The paper presents a relatively modest, albeit clever, improvement on existing linear attention mechanisms.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The core idea of using a logarithmically growing set of hidden states managed by a Fenwick tree is conceptually interesting and provides a mechanism for multi-scale context modeling within linear attention.
    *   The derivation of a parallel training algorithm with log-linear compute complexity is a valuable contribution, making the mechanism potentially practical for long sequences.
    *   The application to existing architectures like Mamba-2 and Gated DeltaNet provides concrete examples of its potential.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The performance improvements, while present, appear somewhat inconsistent and marginal in several benchmarks (particularly in the LongBench tasks). Many tasks exhibit no improvements at all. Furthermore, on the needle-in-a-haystack tasks, Mamba-2 performance with log-linear attention actually performs *worse* on certain key-value pairs. This suggests that the benefits are not universally applicable.
    *   The paper acknowledges that there's still a performance gap compared to standard Transformers, which undermines the claims of comparable performance.
    *   The engineering complexity of the approach is higher than standard linear attention, which could limit its widespread adoption. It is not clear how straightforward it is to integrate with other architectures.
    *   The paper claims subquadratic training, but only subquadratic *with* a chunksize, implying some quadratic operations may still be present within chunks.

*   **Overall Assessment:** The paper introduces a technically sound approach to improve linear attention, but the experimental results don't consistently demonstrate substantial improvements across a range of tasks. The inconsistent results and incremental nature of the performance gains make it difficult to classify this work as a significant breakthrough. Further, given the observed reduction in performance of Mamba-2 when log-linear attention is applied in needle-in-a-haystack retrieval on certain key-value pairs, this casts some doubt on the robustness and overall effectiveness of the approach. The complexity of the implementation may further hinder adoption.

- **Score**: 5/10

### GOLFer: Smaller LM-Generated Documents Hallucination Filter & Combiner for Query Expansion in Information Retrieval
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04762v1)
- **Authors**: Lingyuan Liu, Mengxiang Zhang
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs)-based query expansion for information retrieval augments queries with generated hypothetical documents with LLMs. However, its performance relies heavily on the scale of the language models (LMs), necessitating larger, more advanced LLMs. This approach is costly, computationally intensive, and often has limited accessibility. To address these limitations, we introduce GOLFer - Smaller LMs-Generated Documents Hallucination Filter & Combiner - a novel method leveraging smaller open-source LMs for query expansion. GOLFer comprises two modules: a hallucination filter and a documents combiner. The former detects and removes non-factual and inconsistent sentences in generated documents, a common issue with smaller LMs, while the latter combines the filtered content with the query using a weight vector to balance their influence. We evaluate GOLFer alongside dominant LLM-based query expansion methods on three web search and ten low-resource datasets. Experimental results demonstrate that GOLFer consistently outperforms other methods using smaller LMs, and maintains competitive performance against methods using large-size LLMs, demonstrating its effectiveness.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces GOLFer, a novel method for query expansion in information retrieval (IR) that utilizes smaller, open-source language models (LLMs).  Unlike existing LLM-based query expansion techniques that rely on large and computationally expensive LLMs, GOLFer addresses the limitations of using smaller LLMs by incorporating a hallucination filter and a document combiner.  The hallucination filter detects and removes non-factual and inconsistent sentences generated by the smaller LLM. The document combiner then intelligently combines the filtered documents with the original query using a weighted approach to balance their influence.  The paper demonstrates through extensive experimentation across web search and low-resource datasets that GOLFer consistently outperforms other methods using smaller LMs and remains competitive with approaches employing larger LLMs. The method is designed to be lightweight and versatile, integrating into existing transformer-based LMs without extra training.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in addressing the practical limitations of LLM-based query expansion. While query expansion using LLMs is not new (HyDE and Query2Doc being the main baselines), GOLFer's approach is innovative in its focus on smaller LLMs, making it more accessible and computationally efficient. The hallucination filter and document combiner are well-designed modules to mitigate the issues commonly associated with smaller LLMs. The method's capacity to perform well against existing methods is noteworthy.

*   **Significance:** The significance of this work is in democratizing access to advanced IR techniques. By enabling query expansion with smaller, open-source LLMs, GOLFer makes these techniques available to a broader range of institutions and researchers who may lack the resources to use large-scale LLMs. The improved factual accuracy of query expansion through the hallucination filter is also a valuable contribution. The results are compelling across a variety of datasets.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The focus on practical accessibility and resource efficiency is a significant strength.
    *   The design of the hallucination filter and document combiner is well-reasoned and effective.
    *   The experiments are comprehensive and cover a wide range of datasets and retrieval models.
    *   The ablation studies provide insights into the importance of each component.
    *   The comparison with HyDE, a popular method using very large LMs is significant.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The hallucination detection method is inherently tied to Transformer-based LLMs due to its reliance on self-attention. This limits its applicability to certain APIs that don't expose these scores. This is explicitly acknowledged as a limitation.
    *   While the paper shows competitive performance against HyDE in several settings, it does fall behind in some low resource settings. It's hard to know the exact reasoning for this behavior, and more discussion of such behavior could further strengthen the analysis.
    *   The optimal values for hyperparameters like the filtering threshold (0.8), the sparse retrieval query repetition (20), and the contribution rate beta for dense retrieval (0.6) are determined empirically and are not tuned on a dataset-specific basis, which might limit generalizability.

*   **Potential Influence:** GOLFer has the potential to significantly impact the field by making LLM-based query expansion more accessible and practical. It could foster the development of more resource-efficient IR systems and promote the adoption of these techniques in low-resource environments. Future work might focus on extending the hallucination detection beyond Transformer-based models and further optimizing the component modules.

*Rigorous Rationale:*

The paper tackles a significant challenge in LLM-based query expansion - its reliance on large, computationally intensive models. By offering a solution that leverages smaller, open-source LLMs without compromising on performance, it addresses a key barrier to adoption. The core innovation resides in the hallucination filter and document combiner modules, which enhance the reliability and accuracy of the expanded query. The comprehensive evaluation across diverse datasets and retrieval models demonstrates the robustness and effectiveness of the proposed approach. Despite some limitations related to the reliance on Transformer models for hallucination detection and the need to empirically determine hyperparameters, the strengths of the paper outweigh its weaknesses, making it a valuable contribution to the field of information retrieval.

### OpenGT: A Comprehensive Benchmark For Graph Transformers
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04765v1)
- **Authors**: Jiachen Tang, Zhonghao Wang, Sirui Chen, Sheng Zhou, Jiawei Chen, Jiajun Bu
- **Abstract**: Graph Transformers (GTs) have recently demonstrated remarkable performance across diverse domains. By leveraging attention mechanisms, GTs are capable of modeling long-range dependencies and complex structural relationships beyond local neighborhoods. However, their applicable scenarios are still underexplored, this highlights the need to identify when and why they excel. Furthermore, unlike GNNs, which predominantly rely on message-passing mechanisms, GTs exhibit a diverse design space in areas such as positional encoding, attention mechanisms, and graph-specific adaptations. Yet, it remains unclear which of these design choices are truly effective and under what conditions. As a result, the community currently lacks a comprehensive benchmark and library to promote a deeper understanding and further development of GTs. To address this gap, this paper introduces OpenGT, a comprehensive benchmark for Graph Transformers. OpenGT enables fair comparisons and multidimensional analysis by establishing standardized experimental settings and incorporating a broad selection of state-of-the-art GNNs and GTs. Our benchmark evaluates GTs from multiple perspectives, encompassing diverse tasks and datasets with varying properties. Through extensive experiments, our benchmark has uncovered several critical insights, including the difficulty of transferring models across task levels, the limitations of local attention, the efficiency trade-offs in several models, the application scenarios of specific positional encodings, and the preprocessing overhead of some positional encodings. We aspire for this work to establish a foundation for future graph transformer research emphasizing fairness, reproducibility, and generalizability. We have developed an easy-to-use library OpenGT for training and evaluating existing GTs. The benchmark code is available at https://github.com/eaglelab-zju/OpenGT.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces OpenGT, a comprehensive benchmark for Graph Transformers (GTs). It aims to address the lack of a unified framework for implementing, comparing, and analyzing GTs in a standardized setting. OpenGT provides a library of implemented GT and GNN models, a diverse suite of datasets, and standardized experimental settings to facilitate fair comparisons and multidimensional analysis. Through extensive experiments, the benchmark identifies critical insights regarding the effectiveness, limitations, and computational behavior of various GT designs, including the role of positional encodings, attention mechanisms, and model scalability. The paper also outlines future research directions based on these findings.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Addressing a Gap:** The paper directly tackles a recognized need within the graph learning community. The lack of standardized benchmarks and libraries makes it difficult to compare GT models effectively, hindering progress.
    *   **Comprehensive Scope:** The OpenGT benchmark encompasses a diverse set of GT models, GNN baselines, datasets (varying in size, homophily, and task type), and experimental settings. This comprehensive nature allows for a more nuanced understanding of GT performance under different conditions.
    *   **Detailed Analysis and Insights:** The paper goes beyond simply presenting performance numbers. It provides insightful observations regarding the strengths and weaknesses of different GT architectures, the impact of positional encodings, and the trade-offs between accuracy and efficiency.
    *   **Unified Framework:** The reliance on the well-established `torch_geometric.graphgym` framework provides a solid modular foundation, promoting reproducibility and extensibility.
    *   **Practical Utility:** The release of the OpenGT library makes the benchmark accessible to the research community, facilitating future research and development in GTs.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Incremental Novelty of Models Implemented:** While comprehensive, the benchmark primarily *re-implements* existing GT models. The *algorithmic* novelty, if any, primarily lies in the benchmark framework and analysis methodology rather than introducing groundbreaking new GT architectures.
    *   **Dataset Selection Justification:** While the paper mentions dataset properties (homophily, sparsity), the justification for *why* these specific datasets were chosen could be stronger. A more detailed discussion of their relevance to real-world graph applications would be beneficial.
    *   **Limited Novel Analysis Methodology:** The research questions, although important, could be seen as somewhat standard benchmark experiments in a new model space.  The *analysis* methods (e.g., ablations, comparisons across groups of models) are reasonable, but do not necessarily push the envelope in evaluation methodology.
    *   **No new datasets/tasks:** The creation of a benchmark provides the opportunity to define new dataset or benchmarks related to particular applications which current GT models find challenging, or may be able to unlock.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    The *primary* novelty lies in creating a well-structured, comprehensive, and easily accessible benchmark and analysis framework for Graph Transformers. The individual components (models, datasets) are mostly existing, but their integration and systematic analysis *within a controlled environment* provides value to the community. This enables researchers to more effectively explore the model and design space of GTs, and may lead to better architectures/design paradigms to emerge.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign the paper a score of **7**.

*   **Rationale:**
    *   It is more than merely incremental (score would be lower), but does not make substantial theoretical contributions in the new GT model design.
    *   The *breadth* of the benchmark (datasets, models, experimental scope) and the insights derived from the analysis are valuable. This is particularly important, given the current proliferation of publications in the graph learning space that are not easily reproducible.
    *   The open-source nature of the OpenGT library ensures its accessibility and promotes reproducibility, which is a significant strength.
    *   The paper addresses an important need in the community, and the benchmark has the potential to significantly influence future GT research by providing a common ground for comparison and analysis.
    *   The analysis methodology employed is not radically novel, but it is rigorous and well-executed, providing a solid foundation for future extensions.

The paper's value comes from the methodical approach of creating a useful, reproducible framework for future GT research. This will likely lower the barriers for other scientists to develop more models, since this framework is already created.

### Fine-Grained Interpretation of Political Opinions in Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04774v1)
- **Authors**: Jingyu Hu, Mengyue Yang, Mengnan Du, Weiru Liu
- **Abstract**: Studies of LLMs' political opinions mainly rely on evaluations of their open-ended responses. Recent work indicates that there is a misalignment between LLMs' responses and their internal intentions. This motivates us to probe LLMs' internal mechanisms and help uncover their internal political states. Additionally, we found that the analysis of LLMs' political opinions often relies on single-axis concepts, which can lead to concept confounds. In this work, we extend the single-axis to multi-dimensions and apply interpretable representation engineering techniques for more transparent LLM political concept learning. Specifically, we designed a four-dimensional political learning framework and constructed a corresponding dataset for fine-grained political concept vector learning. These vectors can be used to detect and intervene in LLM internals. Experiments are conducted on eight open-source LLMs with three representation engineering techniques. Results show these vectors can disentangle political concept confounds. Detection tasks validate the semantic meaning of the vectors and show good generalization and robustness in OOD settings. Intervention Experiments show these vectors can intervene in LLMs to generate responses with different political leanings.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of analyzing and interpreting political opinions within large language models (LLMs). It argues that existing methods often rely on single-axis (left-right) evaluations, leading to concept confounds and neglecting the internal intentions of LLMs. To overcome these limitations, the authors propose a fine-grained political learning framework that extends the analysis to four dimensions (economic, diplomatic, civil, and society). They construct a corresponding dataset and apply interpretable representation engineering techniques (CAA, RepE, Linear Probing) to learn disentangled political concept vectors from LLM internals. The effectiveness of these vectors is evaluated through detection and intervention tasks, demonstrating their ability to identify political leanings and steer LLM outputs in a desired direction. The study is conducted on eight open-source LLMs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper introduces a novel multi-dimensional framework for analyzing political opinions in LLMs. Shifting from a single left-right axis to a four-dimensional approach is a significant conceptual improvement and addresses a key limitation in prior work. The application of representation engineering techniques specifically to the fine-grained analysis of political views within LLMs is also relatively novel.

*   **Significance:** The research contributes to the growing field of LLM interpretability and bias detection. By providing a more nuanced understanding of how political concepts are encoded within LLMs, the paper offers valuable insights into the potential biases and influences that these models may exhibit. The ability to intervene and steer LLM outputs based on these disentangled concepts has implications for mitigating undesirable biases and promoting more balanced and representative responses. The practical framework and code/data resources are reusable and extendable, promoting adoption for subsequent research.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Conceptual Framework:** The multi-dimensional framework is a strong contribution, addressing the limitations of simpler single-axis approaches.
    *   **Methodological Rigor:** The use of multiple representation engineering techniques and thorough evaluation through detection and intervention tasks provides a solid methodological foundation.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** Experiments conducted on a range of open-source LLMs demonstrate the generalizability of the findings.
    *   **Dataset Contribution:** Construction of the fine-grained political dataset addresses concept confounds, enhances transparency and meticulosity, and extends single-axis political analysis.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dataset Bias:** While the authors aim to reduce concept confounds, the dataset itself might still contain inherent biases reflecting dominant viewpoints and cultural contexts (U.S. news). Although 970 news headlines are included, it is not evident that those headlines are equally selected for all dimensions, models, and templates.
    *   **Complexity of Intervention:** The intervention experiments, while promising, could benefit from a more in-depth analysis of the generated outputs. While the general direction is demonstrated, there is still some unpredictability in LLM responses, and understanding the nuances of these changes warrants further investigation.
    *   **Limited Theoretical Grounding:** The paper can expand to discuss related studies in political psychology and sociological theory regarding political dimensions to further solidify the four-dimensional framework.

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence the development of more transparent, controllable, and unbiased LLMs. The framework and techniques proposed can be used by researchers and practitioners to identify and mitigate political biases in these models, as well as to steer LLM outputs in a desired direction.

*   **Score Justification:**

    The paper makes a significant contribution to the field of LLM interpretability and bias detection, primarily because of the novel multi-dimensional analysis framework and systematic application of representation engineering to political orientations of LLMs. The results are well-supported by the experimental setup and evaluations, and the weaknesses are acknowledged and can be addressed in future research. While the dataset and intervention strategies could be further refined, the overall contribution is valuable and has the potential to influence future research and development in this area.

- **Score**: 7/10

### MMSU: A Massive Multi-task Spoken Language Understanding and Reasoning Benchmark
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04779v1)
- **Authors**: Dingdong Wang, Jincenzi Wu, Junan Li, Dongchao Yang, Xueyuan Chen, Tianhua Zhang, Helen Meng
- **Abstract**: Speech inherently contains rich acoustic information that extends far beyond the textual language. In real-world spoken language understanding, effective interpretation often requires integrating semantic meaning (e.g., content), paralinguistic features (e.g., emotions, speed, pitch) and phonological characteristics (e.g., prosody, intonation, rhythm), which are embedded in speech. While recent multimodal Speech Large Language Models (SpeechLLMs) have demonstrated remarkable capabilities in processing audio information, their ability to perform fine-grained perception and complex reasoning in natural speech remains largely unexplored. To address this gap, we introduce MMSU, a comprehensive benchmark designed specifically for understanding and reasoning in spoken language. MMSU comprises 5,000 meticulously curated audio-question-answer triplets across 47 distinct tasks. To ground our benchmark in linguistic theory, we systematically incorporate a wide range of linguistic phenomena, including phonetics, prosody, rhetoric, syntactics, semantics, and paralinguistics. Through a rigorous evaluation of 14 advanced SpeechLLMs, we identify substantial room for improvement in existing models, highlighting meaningful directions for future optimization. MMSU establishes a new standard for comprehensive assessment of spoken language understanding, providing valuable insights for developing more sophisticated human-AI speech interaction systems. MMSU benchmark is available at https://huggingface.co/datasets/ddwang2000/MMSU. Evaluation Code is available at https://github.com/dingdongwang/MMSU_Bench.
- **Summary**: The paper introduces MMSU, a new benchmark for evaluating spoken language understanding and reasoning in Speech Large Language Models (SpeechLLMs). MMSU consists of 5,000 curated audio-question-answer triplets across 47 tasks, grounded in linguistic theory and covering phonetics, prosody, rhetoric, syntax, semantics, and paralinguistics. The paper evaluates 14 advanced SpeechLLMs, revealing significant room for improvement, particularly in interpreting paralinguistic and prosodic cues.

**Rigorous and Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Scope:** MMSU addresses a crucial gap by providing a comprehensive benchmark for spoken language understanding, encompassing a wide range of linguistic phenomena often overlooked by existing benchmarks.
    *   **Linguistic Grounding:** The benchmark's design, based on established linguistic principles, is a significant step forward, providing a theoretically sound evaluation framework.
    *   **Diverse Tasks:** The inclusion of 47 distinct tasks covering perception and reasoning abilities makes MMSU a versatile tool for assessing different aspects of SLU.
    *   **Real-world Data:** The reliance on authentic audio samples (sourced from open datasets and professional recordings) enhances the ecological validity of the benchmark.
    *   **Rigorous Evaluation:** The paper provides a thorough evaluation of 14 SpeechLLMs, offering valuable insights into their strengths and weaknesses.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Custom Data:** While the benchmark prioritizes real-world recordings, a relatively small portion (13.44%) is custom recorded, potentially limiting the control over specific linguistic features.
    *   **Potential for Bias:** Although expert-reviewed, there is a potential for annotation bias in a curated dataset.
    *   **Scalability:** The manual curation and review process is time-consuming and may limit the scalability of the benchmark.
    *   **Limited to English:** The benchmark seems to be heavily focused on English language datasets.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    *   MMSU is novel in its comprehensive coverage of linguistic phenomena and its integration of established linguistic principles into the benchmark design. It establishes a new standard for comprehensive assessment of spoken language understanding.

*   **Potential Influence:**

    *   MMSU can significantly influence the field by providing a standardized evaluation framework, enabling fair comparisons between models and guiding future research directions. The identification of limitations in existing models can spur innovation in SpeechLLMs.

**Justification:**

MMSU represents a significant contribution to the field by addressing the limitations of existing benchmarks and providing a comprehensive, linguistically grounded evaluation framework for spoken language understanding. While there are some limitations regarding data control and potential for bias, the strengths of MMSU outweigh these weaknesses. The potential for influencing future research directions and driving innovation in SpeechLLMs is substantial.

### Towards LLM-Centric Multimodal Fusion: A Survey on Integration Strategies and Techniques
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04788v1)
- **Authors**: Jisu An, Junseok Lee, Jeoungeun Lee, Yongseok Son
- **Abstract**: The rapid progress of Multimodal Large Language Models(MLLMs) has transformed the AI landscape. These models combine pre-trained LLMs with various modality encoders. This integration requires a systematic understanding of how different modalities connect to the language backbone. Our survey presents an LLM-centric analysis of current approaches. We examine methods for transforming and aligning diverse modal inputs into the language embedding space. This addresses a significant gap in existing literature. We propose a classification framework for MLLMs based on three key dimensions. First, we examine architectural strategies for modality integration. This includes both the specific integration mechanisms and the fusion level. Second, we categorize representation learning techniques as either joint or coordinate representations. Third, we analyze training paradigms, including training strategies and objective functions. By examining 125 MLLMs developed between 2021 and 2025, we identify emerging patterns in the field. Our taxonomy provides researchers with a structured overview of current integration techniques. These insights aim to guide the development of more robust multimodal integration strategies for future models built on pre-trained foundations.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and critical evaluation of the survey paper based on the provided text.

**Summary:**

The survey paper "Towards LLM-Centric Multimodal Fusion: A Survey on Integration Strategies and Techniques" aims to provide a structured analysis of how multimodal large language models (MLLMs) integrate different modalities (images, audio, video) with the language backbone of LLMs. The paper proposes a classification framework based on three key dimensions: (1) architectural strategies for modality fusion (abstraction, projection, semantic embedding, cross-attention, and fusion levels), (2) representation learning paradigms (joint or coordinate representations), and (3) training methodologies (training strategies and objective functions). By analyzing 125 MLLM papers, the survey identifies emerging patterns, clarifies the functions of common architectural components, and aims to guide future research in building more robust multimodal integration strategies. The paper emphasizes its LLM-centric approach, focusing on how modalities are adapted to the language embedding space, and highlights the often-overlooked contextual roles of architectural components within the integration process. It offers a taxonomy of fusion mechanisms and a comprehensive comparison of existing MLLM studies.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its comprehensive classification framework, particularly its emphasis on the contextual understanding of architectural components and their relationship to LLM embedding spaces. Previous surveys have tended to focus on components, datasets and training but often miss the varying function and impact the same archtiecture has across different setups.The distinction between abstraction, projection, semantic embedding, and cross-attention as contextual fusion mechanisms is a valuable contribution. The identified fusion levels (early, intermediate, hybrid) are useful, though not entirely new, but the paper's integration of these levels with the other dimensions makes it more insightful.

*   **Significance:** The significance of this survey comes from the need to understand the rapidly growing field of MLLMs. By providing a structured overview of integration techniques, the survey helps researchers navigate the complex landscape of MLLM architectures and identify promising avenues for future research. The survey attempts to bridge the understanding of functionality versus archicture to reduce initial investment in understanding of new models by other researches.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Comprehensive Coverage: Analysis of 125 papers is a strong foundation.
    *   Clear Taxonomy: The three-dimensional classification framework is well-defined and helpful.
    *   LLM-Centric View: The emphasis on aligning modalities with the LLM embedding space is insightful.
    *   Contextual Analysis: Focus on the varying roles of architectural components across different models is a key strength.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Qualitative Focus: The analysis is primarily qualitative, lacking quantitative comparisons or empirical evaluations of different integration techniques. This limits the ability to draw definitive conclusions about the effectiveness of various approaches.
    *   Limited Scope: While comprehensive, the survey could benefit from further discussion on emerging trends such as continual learning in MLLMs and their application across diverse domains (e.g., robotics, healthcare).
    *   Subjectivity: The classification of architectural components and fusion levels may be subjective in some cases, potentially leading to ambiguities or disagreements in interpretation.
    *   Incremental Novelty: While the contextual view is important, the novelty is incremental. It expands on existing surveys rather than introducing entirely new concepts.

*   **Potential Influence:** The survey has the potential to influence future research by providing a structured framework for analyzing MLLM architectures and guiding the development of more effective integration strategies. It provides a valuable resource for researchers entering the field and helps to clarify the key design choices in MLLM development. The breakdown of the model architectures into separate components helps to abstract to individual function and understand where to focus efforts.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:** The paper makes a solid contribution by offering a structured and comprehensive overview of MLLM integration strategies. Its LLM-centric approach and emphasis on contextual understanding of architectural components are valuable strengths. However, the lack of quantitative analysis, the potentially subjective classifications, and incremental novelty temper the overall score. While the paper offers a helpful guide to the current state of MLLM integration, it doesn't revolutionize the field but provides useful insights.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Dissecting Logical Reasoning in LLMs: A Fine-Grained Evaluation and Supervision Study
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04810v1)
- **Authors**: Yujun Zhou, Jiayi Ye, Zipeng Ling, Yufei Han, Yue Huang, Haomin Zhuang, Zhenwen Liang, Kehan Guo, Taicheng Guo, Xiangqi Wang, Xiangliang Zhang
- **Abstract**: Logical reasoning is a core capability for many applications of large language models (LLMs), yet existing benchmarks often rely solely on final-answer accuracy, failing to capture the quality and structure of the reasoning process. We propose FineLogic, a fine-grained evaluation framework that assesses logical reasoning across three dimensions: overall benchmark accuracy, stepwise soundness, and representation-level alignment. In addition, to better understand how reasoning capabilities emerge, we conduct a comprehensive study on the effects of supervision format during fine-tuning. We construct four supervision styles (one natural language and three symbolic variants) and train LLMs under each. Our findings reveal that natural language supervision yields strong generalization even on out-of-distribution and long-context tasks, while symbolic reasoning styles promote more structurally sound and atomic inference chains. Further, our representation-level probing shows that fine-tuning primarily improves reasoning behaviors through step-by-step generation, rather than enhancing shortcut prediction or internalized correctness. Together, our framework and analysis provide a more rigorous and interpretable lens for evaluating and improving logical reasoning in LLMs.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces FineLogic, a novel evaluation framework for assessing logical reasoning in large language models (LLMs). FineLogic goes beyond simple final-answer accuracy by evaluating reasoning across three dimensions: overall accuracy, stepwise soundness (validity, relevance, and atomicity of intermediate steps), and representation-level alignment (probing internal representations).  The authors then use FineLogic to conduct a study on the impact of different supervision formats (natural language vs. symbolic) during fine-tuning.  Their findings suggest natural language supervision yields strong generalization, while symbolic reasoning formats promote more structured and atomic inference chains.  The representation-level probing reveals that fine-tuning mainly improves step-by-step generation rather than shortcut prediction or internalized correctness.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Strengths:**
    * **Novel Evaluation Framework:**  FineLogic addresses a significant gap in LLM evaluation by moving beyond final answer accuracy. The stepwise soundness and representation-level probing provide much richer insights into the reasoning process.  The proposed relevance and atomicity measures for stepwise soundness are significant contributions.
    * **Comprehensive Study:**  The systematic comparison of different supervision formats is well-executed. The authors explore a good range of options (natural language, structured symbolic, filtered symbolic, direct symbolic) and analyze the trade-offs between them. The use of probing techniques is also effective in providing insights into how supervision influences internal representations.
    * **Practical Implications:** The findings offer valuable guidance for designing training strategies for improving LLMs' logical reasoning abilities. Knowing that natural language supervision generalizes well while symbolic promotes better structure allows for more informed training data selection.
    * **Well-Organized and Clear Presentation:** The paper is structured logically, with clear research questions, methodologies, and results. The figures and tables effectively present the data. The limitations section is candid and highlights opportunities for future work.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Reliance on GPT-4.1-mini for Validity and Atomicity Evaluation:** While the authors claim high accuracy (98%) for GPT-4.1-mini in evaluating validity and atomicity, any error rate in this process will influence the credibility of the overall step-wise soundness measurement. This introduces a potential bias. The manual verification only involves 200 steps, so more verification should be done.
    * **Limited Scope of Representation-Level Probing:** The representation-level probing is conducted only on the LLaMA and Qwen models. Extending this analysis to other models or model sizes would strengthen the generalizability of the findings. It only covers one model type for SFT.
    * **Incremental Contribution (Evaluation Framework):** Step-wise evaluation and representation-level probing, in a broader sense, are not entirely new. Saparov and He (2022) introduced step-wise evaluation. And representation-level probing has also been introduced in previous works. The increment is the incorporation of relevant, and atomicity measures for stepwise soundness evaluation. The contribution to representation-level probing may have already been available in previous work.
    * **Simplified Symbolic Representations:** Direct translation from the raw corpus can be difficult to perform.

* **Novelty and Significance:**

The paper offers a significant contribution to the field of LLM reasoning by providing a more fine-grained evaluation framework than existing benchmarks. While the individual components of FineLogic (stepwise evaluation, representation probing) have been explored before, the combination of these methods, along with a specific focus on logical reasoning and a detailed analysis of supervision formats, is novel. The findings regarding the trade-offs between natural language and symbolic supervision are valuable and have the potential to influence future research in this area. However, the impact is somewhat reduced by the reliance on GPT for soundness and atomicity checks which could be viewed with slight skepticism.
The paper does contribute by providing a more rigorous and interpretable lens for evaluating and improving logical reasoning in LLMs by providing concrete insights into the design and selection of effective training data for supervised logical reasoning.

**Score: 7.5**

**Justification:**

The paper's strengths lie in its well-designed evaluation framework and comprehensive analysis of supervision formats. FineLogic is a valuable tool for understanding LLM reasoning, and the findings provide practical guidance for improving reasoning abilities. However, the reliance on GPT-4.1-mini for evaluating validity and atomicity, along with the limited scope of representation-level probing, detracts from its overall significance. Furthermore, some aspects of the framework draw from previous work, reducing the overall degree of innovation. Nevertheless, the comprehensive nature of the investigation, along with the insightful findings regarding the trade-offs between supervision formats, makes it a valuable contribution that warrants a good score. The novelty and significance are present, but not transformative enough to justify a higher score. The limitations are also a point to consider, which prevents a score of 8 or above.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Design of intelligent proofreading system for English translation based on CNN and BERT
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04811v1)
- **Authors**: Feijun Liu, Huifeng Wang, Kun Wang, Yizhen Wang
- **Abstract**: Since automatic translations can contain errors that require substantial human post-editing, machine translation proofreading is essential for improving quality. This paper proposes a novel hybrid approach for robust proofreading that combines convolutional neural networks (CNN) with Bidirectional Encoder Representations from Transformers (BERT). In order to extract semantic information from phrases and expressions, CNN uses a variety of convolution kernel filters to capture local n-gram patterns. In the meanwhile, BERT creates context-rich representations of whole sequences by utilizing stacked bidirectional transformer encoders. Using BERT's attention processes, the integrated error detection component relates tokens to spot translation irregularities including word order problems and omissions. The correction module then uses parallel English-German alignment and GRU decoder models in conjunction with translation memory to propose logical modifications that maintain original meaning. A unified end-to-end training process optimized for post-editing performance is applied to the whole pipeline. The multi-domain collection of WMT and the conversational dialogues of Open-Subtitles are two of the English-German parallel corpora used to train the model. Multiple loss functions supervise detection and correction capabilities. Experiments attain a 90% accuracy, 89.37% F1, and 16.24% MSE, exceeding recent proofreading techniques by over 10% overall. Comparative benchmarking demonstrates state-of-the-art performance in identifying and coherently rectifying mistranslations and omissions.
- **Summary**: Okay, I've analyzed the provided OCR of the research paper and am ready to summarize, evaluate, and score it.

**Summary:**

The paper presents a hybrid sequence modeling approach for English translation proofreading, combining Convolutional Neural Networks (CNN) and Bidirectional Encoder Representations from Transformers (BERT). The CNN extracts local n-gram features, while BERT captures context-rich representations of whole sequences. An error detection component uses BERT's attention mechanisms to identify translation irregularities, including word order problems and omissions. A correction module utilizes parallel English-German alignment and a GRU decoder with translation memory to suggest meaning-preserving modifications. The model is trained end-to-end on WMT and OpenSubtitles corpora, supervised by multiple loss functions. The results demonstrate improvements in accuracy, F1-score, and MSE compared to existing proofreading techniques.  The paper claims state-of-the-art performance in identifying and correcting mistranslations and omissions and suggests future directions including multilingual expansion and commercial integration.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Hybrid Approach:** The combination of CNN and BERT is a reasonable approach. The CNN component should be effective at extracting local features and BERT at capturing the global context. This architecture aims to leverage the strengths of both models to improve translation quality and coherence.
*   **End-to-End Training:** Training the entire pipeline end-to-end allows for optimizing the individual components for the overall task of proofreading, which is often more effective than training them separately.
*   **Evaluation Datasets:** Using standard datasets like WMT and OpenSubtitles allows for comparison with existing methods and ensures the generalizability of the results.
*   **Performance Metrics:** The paper presents a range of evaluation metrics (accuracy, F1, MSE, BLEU) that offer a relatively comprehensive view of the model's performance.
*   **Clear Architecture:** The description of the model architecture, including the roles of CNN, BERT, GRU, and translation memory, is relatively clear.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Novelty:** The combination of CNNs and BERT is becoming increasingly common in NLP tasks. The novelty in this paper is relatively weak and the contribution appears incremental rather than groundbreaking. Just combining two existing models doesn't automatically make it novel; the method of combination and its specific tuning is critical. The paper lacks details about this tuning.
*   **Lack of in-depth architecture novelty details:** The descriptions of each module are general. More details on how the CNN and BERT models are specifically configured, how the GRU is used, and how the translation memory is integrated would add weight to the claim of innovation and the reproducibility of the solution.
*   **Limited Ablation Studies:** There is a lack of adequate ablation studies. Does each component actually improve the model performance? This is a crucial element to justify the model's construction and to demonstrate that all components are necessary and helpful.
*   **Modest Performance Gains:** While the paper claims improvements, the reported percentage gains alone do not sufficiently demonstrate the real-world impact of the method. The actual improvement of the translation quality from a user perspective is not sufficiently established. More information regarding state-of-the-art comparisons would be useful for determining the significance of the model's improvement.
*   **Results Presentation and Analysis:** The results are presented as tables and figures related to hyperparameters or dataset batch size changes. No comparison against state-of-the-art models from other research is conducted.
*   **Lack of code/resources:** Providing access to code or at least releasing a demonstration would dramatically increase the impact of this research.
*   **Limited error analysis** - while the paper states that one was conducted, it is not detailed or explained.
*    **Limited technical depth:** While the paper presents mathematical formulas, its technical depth is superficial. It lacks a deeper theoretical analysis of the proposed approach.

**Significance:**

The paper's significance is moderate. While the authors claim significant improvements, the lack of comparison with previous research, or a detailed error analysis leaves a feeling of wanting to see more compelling evidence of impact. Given the increasing importance of machine translation, any method that substantially improves quality has the potential to contribute. However, there are weaknesses that temper the model's potential significance.

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning a score of **5**.

*   The hybrid approach, though not entirely novel, is a reasonable strategy.
*   The end-to-end training and use of standard datasets are positive.
*   However, the limited novelty, lack of comparative analysis with other studies, and incomplete evaluation significantly impact the score. The performance claims, though seemingly positive, are not thoroughly substantiated against a broader research landscape.
*   The lack of code or access makes it hard to verify the research.
*   The results are also not very compelling in that the method is not compared directly against state-of-the-art solutions.

Score: 5

- **Score**: 5/10

### LogicPuzzleRL: Cultivating Robust Mathematical Reasoning in LLMs via Reinforcement Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04821v1)
- **Authors**: Zhen Hao Wong, Jingwen Deng, Runming He, Zirong Chen, Qijie You, Hejun Dong, Hao Liang, Chengyu Shen, Bin Cui, Wentao Zhang
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) excel at many supervised tasks but often struggle with structured reasoning in unfamiliar settings. This discrepancy suggests that standard fine-tuning pipelines may instill narrow, domain-specific heuristics rather than fostering general-purpose thinking strategies. In this work, we propose a "play to learn" framework that fine-tunes LLMs through reinforcement learning on a suite of seven custom logic puzzles, each designed to cultivate distinct reasoning skills such as constraint propagation, spatial consistency, and symbolic deduction. Using a reinforcement learning setup with verifiable rewards, models receive binary feedback based on puzzle correctness, encouraging iterative, hypothesis-driven problem solving. We demonstrate that this training approach significantly improves out-of-distribution performance on a range of mathematical benchmarks, especially for mid-difficulty problems that require multi-step reasoning. Analyses across problem categories and difficulty levels reveal that puzzle training promotes transferable reasoning routines, strengthening algebraic manipulation, geometric inference, and combinatorial logic, while offering limited gains on rote or highly specialized tasks. These findings show that reinforcement learning over logic puzzles reshapes the internal reasoning of LLMs, enabling more robust and compositional generalization without relying on task-specific symbolic tools.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "LogicPuzzleRL: Cultivating Robust Mathematical Reasoning in LLMs via Reinforcement Learning" investigates using reinforcement learning (RL) on a suite of custom-designed logic puzzles to improve the mathematical reasoning abilities of Large Language Models (LLMs).  The authors created seven different puzzle types, each targeting specific reasoning skills such as constraint propagation, spatial consistency, and symbolic deduction.  They train LLMs using RL with verifiable rewards based on puzzle correctness. The results show that this approach enhances out-of-distribution performance on various mathematical benchmarks, particularly for mid-difficulty problems. The authors perform detailed analyses of reasoning traces to demonstrate that the gains are due to improved reasoning ability rather than the accumulation of task-specific heuristics.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The idea of using specifically designed logic puzzles as a curriculum for training LLMs' reasoning abilities is somewhat novel. While RL has been applied to both game-playing and mathematical reasoning before, the combination of custom, verifiable-reward puzzles targeting specific reasoning skills, along with a detailed analysis of reasoning traces, is a contribution. The analysis differentiating between improved reasoning vs. simply memorizing puzzle solutions is also a key element that reinforces the paper's claims.
* **Significance:** The paper addresses a significant issue: the limitations of standard fine-tuning methods in imparting robust, generalizable reasoning skills to LLMs. The results suggest that carefully designed puzzles can indeed improve out-of-distribution performance on mathematical problems. If the findings are robust and generalizable to other LLM architectures and puzzle sets, it could influence the design of more effective training strategies.
* **Strengths:**
    *   **Well-defined Problem and Approach:** The paper clearly articulates the problem (lack of generalizable reasoning in LLMs) and proposes a structured solution using a puzzle-based RL framework.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper features a thorough evaluation across diverse mathematical benchmarks, difficulty levels, and analysis of intermediate reasoning steps. The ablations of puzzle archetypes allow the reader to understand what skills are being instilled via the various training tasks.
    *   **Verifiable Rewards:** The use of verifiable rewards is a smart design choice, ensuring clear feedback signals during RL training.
    *   **Analysis of Reasoning Traces:** The detailed analysis of reasoning traces to distinguish between improved reasoning and memorization is a significant strength.

* **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Puzzle Design:** While the paper describes the design principles of the puzzles, more detail on the specific implementations and any potential biases inherent in the puzzle creation process would be beneficial.
    *   **Generalizability:** The results might be specific to the chosen LLM architecture and the particular set of puzzles used. While the findings are promising, broader testing and evaluation would strengthen the claims.
    *   **Scale:** It's not entirely clear from the paper the computational cost associated with this fine-tuning technique. Practicality is very important for the field of LLMs, so if training with a large puzzle sets is still computationally infeasible, the impact of this paper could be limited.

**Overall Assessment:**

The paper presents a compelling approach for improving LLMs' reasoning abilities through RL on custom logic puzzles.  The meticulous evaluation and analysis contribute significantly to the credibility of the findings. While the generalizability and potential biases of the puzzle design warrant further investigation, the paper offers valuable insights into fostering robust and transferable reasoning skills in LLMs. I believe the paper offers a solid incremental contribution to the field, moving beyond simply benchmarking existing LLMs and exploring training methods with potential for general reasoning improvements.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:** The paper is more than just incremental - it is novel in its approach and contributes to the literature on LLM reasoning. While not a breakthrough, the methodology is sound and results convincing. The work addresses an important problem (improving reasoning in LLMs) and provides a potentially useful solution. However, it also has some limitations (discussed above) that prevent it from scoring higher.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Evaluating Vision-Language and Large Language Models for Automated Student Assessment in Indonesian Classrooms
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04822v1)
- **Authors**: Nurul Aisyah, Muhammad Dehan Al Kautsar, Arif Hidayat, Raqib Chowdhury, Fajri Koto
- **Abstract**: Although vision-language and large language models (VLM and LLM) offer promising opportunities for AI-driven educational assessment, their effectiveness in real-world classroom settings, particularly in underrepresented educational contexts, remains underexplored. In this study, we evaluated the performance of a state-of-the-art VLM and several LLMs on 646 handwritten exam responses from grade 4 students in six Indonesian schools, covering two subjects: Mathematics and English. These sheets contain more than 14K student answers that span multiple choice, short answer, and essay questions. Assessment tasks include grading these responses and generating personalized feedback. Our findings show that the VLM often struggles to accurately recognize student handwriting, leading to error propagation in downstream LLM grading. Nevertheless, LLM-generated feedback retains some utility, even when derived from imperfect input, although limitations in personalization and contextual relevance persist.
