Specifically, it is equipped with a systematical definition of five requisite skills during the comprehension process, a strict framework to construct testing data for these skills, and a detailed analysis of advanced open-sourced and closed-sourced LLMs using the testing data. With SCOP, we find that it is still challenging for LLMs to perform an expert-level comprehension process. Even so, we notice that LLMs share some similarities with experts, e.g., performing better at comprehending local information than global information. Further analysis reveals that LLMs can be somewhat unreliable -- they might reach correct answers through flawed comprehension processes. Based on SCOP, we suggest that one direction for improving LLMs is to focus more on the comprehension process, ensuring all comprehension skills are thoroughly developed during training.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "SCOP: Evaluating the Comprehension Process of Large Language Models from a Cognitive View":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SCOP (Skills for Comprehension Process), a new benchmark and evaluation framework designed to assess the comprehension processes of Large Language Models (LLMs) from a cognitive perspective. SCOP aims to move beyond simply evaluating answer accuracy and instead delve into *how* LLMs comprehend text. It defines five core skills required for comprehension: Locating, Inferring, Connecting, Organizing, and Selecting. The authors develop a data construction framework to create testing data for these skills, modify existing datasets, and crawl for new ones.  Finally, they analyze the performance of several LLMs (both open-source and closed-source) using SCOP, revealing weaknesses in the LLMs' comprehension process and highlighting the divergence between LLM and human expert comprehension strategies. The results indicate that LLMs struggle with more complex comprehension skills like inference and global understanding, and that they sometimes arrive at correct answers through flawed or shortcutted processes. The paper argues for the importance of focusing on improving the comprehension *process* during LLM training rather than solely focusing on answer accuracy.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in its focus on evaluating the *comprehension process* itself, rather than just the final answer. This is a vital shift from many existing RC benchmarks that treat LLMs as "black boxes." By breaking down comprehension into specific skills and designing tasks to evaluate those skills independently, SCOP provides a more granular and insightful assessment of LLM capabilities. The data generation framework that modifies and augments existing datasets to target specific comprehension skills is also a valuable contribution.

*   **Significance:** SCOP is significant for several reasons:
    *   **Addresses a critical gap:** It acknowledges the limitations of relying solely on answer accuracy in safety-critical applications of LLMs, where understanding the *reasoning* behind an answer is crucial.
    *   **Provides a structured framework:** The five defined comprehension skills offer a practical and intuitive way to understand and improve LLM comprehension.
    *   **Identifies key weaknesses:** The empirical results highlight the deficiencies of current LLMs in performing expert-level comprehension, especially in higher-level skills.
    *   **Offers direction for future research:** By pinpointing the disconnect between LLM and expert comprehension processes, the paper guides future research towards more process-oriented training methods and evaluation metrics.
    *   **Generalizability:** The proposed skills and the evaluation framework are likely generalizable across different domains and document types, enhancing its utility.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Well-defined and motivated framework.
    *   Rigorous data creation methodology.
    *   Clear experimental design and analysis.
    *   Focus on a crucial and often overlooked aspect of LLM evaluation.
    *   The code and datasets are planned to be released.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Dataset Reliance:** While the data modification and crawling approach is solid, reliance on existing RC datasets with associated biases may limit the scope and objectivity of evaluation.
    *   **Prompt sensitivity:** The impact of different prompts on LLM behavior is a known factor. While the authors likely kept prompts simple, this is an area of potential variability.
    *   **Simplified cognitive model:** The five skills, while helpful, represent a simplified abstraction of the human cognitive process of reading comprehension. Finer-grained analysis could be beneficial.
    *   **Lack of explainability:** While the paper focuses on *process*, it doesn't offer a concrete method for making the *internal workings* of LLMs regarding comprehension more interpretable.
    *   **No interventions provided:** The paper identifies deficiencies but does not propose or test interventions to address them.

*   **Potential Influence:** SCOP has the potential to influence how LLMs are trained and evaluated. It can inspire the development of new training techniques that explicitly target the identified comprehension skills and promote more human-aligned reasoning processes. It can also serve as a foundation for future benchmarks that go beyond simple answer accuracy.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution by shifting the focus of LLM evaluation from answer accuracy to the underlying comprehension process. The proposed SCOP framework is well-defined, rigorously evaluated, and provides actionable insights into the strengths and weaknesses of current LLMs. However, the reliance on existing datasets and the lack of concrete interventions to address the identified deficiencies hold it back from a higher score. Therefore, the paper warrants a **strong score but not an exceptional one**.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### QiMeng: Fully Automated Hardware and Software Design for Processor Chip
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05007v1)
- **Authors**: Rui Zhang, Yuanbo Wen, Shuyao Cheng, Di Huang, Shaohui Peng, Jiaming Guo, Pengwei Jin, Jiacheng Zhao, Tianrui Ma, Yaoyu Zhu, Yifan Hao, Yongwei Zhao, Shengwen Liang, Ying Wang, Xing Hu, Zidong Du, Huimin Cui, Ling Li, Qi Guo, Yunji Chen
- **Abstract**: Processor chip design technology serves as a key frontier driving breakthroughs in computer science and related fields. With the rapid advancement of information technology, conventional design paradigms face three major challenges: the physical constraints of fabrication technologies, the escalating demands for design resources, and the increasing diversity of ecosystems. Automated processor chip design has emerged as a transformative solution to address these challenges. While recent breakthroughs in Artificial Intelligence (AI), particularly Large Language Models (LLMs) techniques, have opened new possibilities for fully automated processor chip design, substantial challenges remain in establishing domain-specific LLMs for processor chip design. In this paper, we propose QiMeng, a novel system for fully automated hardware and software design of processor chips. QiMeng comprises three hierarchical layers. In the bottom-layer, we construct a domain-specific Large Processor Chip Model (LPCM) that introduces novel designs in architecture, training, and inference, to address key challenges such as knowledge representation gap, data scarcity, correctness assurance, and enormous solution space. In the middle-layer, leveraging the LPCM's knowledge representation and inference capabilities, we develop the Hardware Design Agent and the Software Design Agent to automate the design of hardware and software for processor chips. Currently, several components of QiMeng have been completed and successfully applied in various top-layer applications, demonstrating significant advantages and providing a feasible solution for efficient, fully automated hardware/software design of processor chips. Future research will focus on integrating all components and performing iterative top-down and bottom-up design processes to establish a comprehensive QiMeng system.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper, "QiMeng: Fully Automated Hardware and Software Design for Processor Chip".

**Summary:**

The paper introduces QiMeng, a novel system for fully automating the hardware and software design of processor chips. It addresses limitations in conventional design paradigms arising from fabrication constraints, resource demands, and ecosystem diversity. QiMeng employs a three-layered architecture: (1) a domain-specific Large Processor Chip Model (LPCM) at the bottom layer, designed to overcome challenges such as knowledge representation gaps, data scarcity, and correctness assurance; (2) Hardware and Software Design Agents in the middle layer, leveraging LPCM to automate hardware and software design respectively; and (3) applications in the top layer, demonstrating the capabilities of the agents. LPCM features novel designs in architecture, training, and inference, while the agents incorporate dual-loop mechanisms for performance optimization and error correction. The paper showcases applications like automated front-end design, HDL generation, OS configuration optimization, and compiler tool-chain design, illustrating the system's potential for efficient and fully automated processor chip design. The authors also outline a phased approach for building QiMeng, transitioning from top-down application development to bottom-up model integration and self-evolution.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The concept of a fully automated hardware and software co-design system for processors is compelling, especially with the integration of LLMs. However, the novelty rests significantly on the implementation details of the LPCM and the design agents. While the paper describes the architecture and overall approach, it lacks detailed algorithmic or mathematical formulations of the LPCM's training, inference, or the dual-loop mechanisms used by the agents. The claim of "novel designs" within the LPCM remains somewhat vague without concrete examples. The paper also needs to show how this approach is fundamentally different from or improves existing HLS and DSE techniques. A discussion how QiMeng can provide new ways for exploring hardware-software co-design to beat human-driven design is necessary.
    For cross-stage collaborative training, there seems to be some similarity with Chain-of-Thought (CoT) imitation learning. A comparison and explaination on how their apprach is different would strengthen the work.

*   **Significance:** The potential impact is substantial. Automating processor chip design could significantly reduce development time and costs, enabling faster innovation and customized solutions. The use of LLMs to bridge the gap between abstract specifications and concrete implementations is promising. The applications section showcasing achievements in automated hardware and software design provides tangible evidence of QiMeng's capabilities. However, the presented "proof of concept" applications still seem to operate in silo, and they should be integrated into the proposed overall system. To further establish QiMeng's impact, the paper needs to provide a clearer integration path for all the demonstrated application to demonstrate the overall effectiveness. The current hardware design is still operating on the old-fashioned EDA.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Ambitious goal:** Addressing the grand challenge of fully automated chip design.
    *   **Comprehensive framework:** Encompassing both hardware and software aspects.
    *   **Integration of AI:** Leveraging LLMs and agents for intelligent automation.
    *   **Practical applications:** Demonstrating tangible results in key design areas.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Lack of technical depth:** Insufficient details on the LPCM's architecture, training, and inference mechanisms.
    *   **Limited quantitative evaluation:**  The performance comparisons (e.g., to Intel 486) are not particularly compelling, and more detailed performance benchmarks are needed to justify the effectiveness and integration of the proposed system.
    *   **Incomplete integration:** Many components are at prototype stage, and comprehensive integration is ongoing.

*   **Justification for Score:**

    The paper presents an interesting vision and framework for fully automated processor chip design. The components and applications showcases show the potential of the proposed paradigm. However, the lack of technical depth, limited quantitative evaluations, and the incomplete integration of the entire framework weaken its immediate impact and practical applicability. The performance is compared to very old Intel processors and needs comparisons against newer and custom design.

    Therefore, a score of 6 seems appropriate.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.AR
- **Score**: 6/10

### Automatic Robustness Stress Testing of LLMs as Mathematical Problem Solvers
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05038v1)
- **Authors**: Yutao Hou, Zeguan Xiao, Fei Yu, Yihan Jiang, Xuetao Wei, Hailiang Huang, Yun Chen, Guanhua Chen
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) have achieved distinguished performance on various reasoning-intensive tasks. However, LLMs might still face the challenges of robustness issues and fail unexpectedly in some simple reasoning tasks. Previous works evaluate the LLM robustness with hand-crafted templates or a limited set of perturbation rules, indicating potential data contamination in pre-training or fine-tuning datasets. In this work, inspired by stress testing in software engineering, we propose a novel framework, Automatic Robustness Checker (AR-Checker), to generate mathematical problem variants that maintain the semantic meanings of the original one but might fail the LLMs. The AR-Checker framework generates mathematical problem variants through multi-round parallel streams of LLM-based rewriting and verification. Our framework can generate benchmark variants dynamically for each LLM, thus minimizing the risk of data contamination. Experiments on GSM8K and MATH-500 demonstrate the strong performance of AR-Checker on mathematical tasks. We also evaluate AR-Checker on benchmarks beyond mathematics, including MMLU, MMLU-Pro, and CommonsenseQA, where it also achieves strong performance, further proving the effectiveness of AR-Checker.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces AR-CHECKER, a novel framework for automatically stress-testing Large Language Models (LLMs) as mathematical problem solvers. Inspired by stress testing in software engineering, AR-CHECKER aims to identify robustness issues in LLMs by generating mathematical problem variants that maintain the semantic meaning of the original problems but are designed to potentially cause the LLMs to fail. The framework consists of three LLMs: a rewriter (to generate problem variants), a verifier (to ensure semantic equivalence and answer correctness), and a target LLM (the one being tested). The rewriter operates in multi-round parallel streams to explore a diverse solution space while minimizing data contamination risks. The paper demonstrates the effectiveness of AR-CHECKER on GSM8K and MATH-500 datasets, showing a significant drop in LLM accuracy after applying the framework. It further evaluates AR-CHECKER on other benchmarks like MMLU, MMLU-Pro, and CommonsenseQA, demonstrating its broad applicability.  The paper explores the impact of various parameters, rewriter model choice, and the transferability of the generated adversarial examples. It also provides an analysis of the types of weaknesses exhibited by the LLMs under stress testing.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of automatically generating adversarial examples for LLMs via a multi-LLM framework is somewhat novel. Previous works have explored robustness evaluation, but AR-CHECKER's dynamic, LLM-driven generation and verification process represents a significant advance over template-based or fixed-rule approaches.  The use of parallel streams for search is also a valuable contribution. The paper's ability to generate diverse adversarial examples minimizes the risk of data contamination, which is a key concern in LLM evaluation.

*   **Significance:** The paper highlights a crucial issue: the lack of robustness in LLMs, even when they appear to perform well on standard benchmarks. AR-CHECKER provides a valuable tool for uncovering these weaknesses and could inform the development of more robust LLMs. The identification of common weakness types (e.g., sequential reasoning failures, over-reliance on peripheral details, numerical variation sensitivity) is also useful for targeted improvement strategies. The scalability demonstrations with larger models support the method's long-term utility.  The broad applicability shown across diverse benchmarks beyond math contributes to a greater impact.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The AR-CHECKER framework is well-defined and explained.
    *   The experimental results convincingly demonstrate AR-CHECKER's effectiveness in reducing LLM accuracy.
    *   The ablation studies and analysis of weakness types offer valuable insights.
    *   The exploration of the framework's scalability and transferability strengthens its practical relevance.
    *   The method minimizes the risk of data contamination.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Reliance on LLMs for rewriting and verification introduces biases and potential for errors. The verifier, although achieving good precision, is not perfect. A potential flaw is that while semantic equivalence is maintained in most cases, the "core meaning" is somewhat vague, and could be subjective.
    *   Computational Cost: The framework requires significant computational resources, making it less accessible than static benchmarks.
    *   While the weakness analysis provides insights, it is still somewhat qualitative. Quantifying the severity and prevalence of each weakness type would be beneficial.
    *   The choice of LLMs for the rewriter, verifier, and target is somewhat arbitrary. While the ablation on the rewriter's performance is discussed, further justification for these model choices could strengthen the analysis.
    *   While the system minimizes data contamination risks, it doesn't eliminate it entirely, as similar reasoning patterns or paraphrasing techniques from the source data could be leveraged.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by:
    *   Promoting the development of more robust LLMs.
    *   Providing a new benchmark for evaluating LLM robustness.
    *   Inspiring further research into automatic adversarial example generation.
    *   Guiding the development of more targeted LLM improvement strategies.

**Justification for Score:**

I'm assigning a score of 7. The paper presents a novel and well-executed approach to stress-testing LLMs, with strong experimental results and valuable insights.  However, the reliance on LLMs for core components (rewriting and verification) introduces potential biases and errors.  The computational cost is also a limitation. While the idea itself is quite strong and the paper provides helpful data, there aren't groundbreaking implications that drastically change the trajectory of the field.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### FlowDirector: Training-Free Flow Steering for Precise Text-to-Video Editing
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05046v1)
- **Authors**: Guangzhao Li, Yanming Yang, Chenxi Song, Chi Zhang
- **Abstract**: Text-driven video editing aims to modify video content according to natural language instructions. While recent training-free approaches have made progress by leveraging pre-trained diffusion models, they typically rely on inversion-based techniques that map input videos into the latent space, which often leads to temporal inconsistencies and degraded structural fidelity. To address this, we propose FlowDirector, a novel inversion-free video editing framework. Our framework models the editing process as a direct evolution in data space, guiding the video via an Ordinary Differential Equation (ODE) to smoothly transition along its inherent spatiotemporal manifold, thereby preserving temporal coherence and structural details. To achieve localized and controllable edits, we introduce an attention-guided masking mechanism that modulates the ODE velocity field, preserving non-target regions both spatially and temporally. Furthermore, to address incomplete edits and enhance semantic alignment with editing instructions, we present a guidance-enhanced editing strategy inspired by Classifier-Free Guidance, which leverages differential signals between multiple candidate flows to steer the editing trajectory toward stronger semantic alignment without compromising structural consistency. Extensive experiments across benchmarks demonstrate that FlowDirector achieves state-of-the-art performance in instruction adherence, temporal consistency, and background preservation, establishing a new paradigm for efficient and coherent video editing without inversion.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "FlowDirector: Training-Free Flow Steering for Precise Text-to-Video Editing":

**Summary:**

The paper presents FlowDirector, a novel training-free framework for text-driven video editing.  It addresses limitations of existing inversion-based methods, which often lead to temporal inconsistencies and structural degradation. FlowDirector models the editing process as a direct evolution in data space guided by an Ordinary Differential Equation (ODE), preserving temporal coherence and structural details.  Key components include: (1) Spatially Attentive Flow Correction (SAFC), which uses attention-guided masking to enable localized and controllable edits; and (2) Differential Averaging Guidance (DAG), inspired by Classifier-Free Guidance, to improve semantic alignment while mitigating the limitations of structural preservation.  Experiments demonstrate state-of-the-art performance in instruction adherence, temporal consistency, and background preservation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper introduces a truly novel approach to text-to-video editing by moving away from the conventional inversion-based techniques that dominate the field. While flow-based methods have been explored in image editing, their application to video and the specific combination of SAFC and DAG for precise and controllable edits are significant innovations.
*   **Inversion-Free Framework**: The core idea of directly evolving the video in data space using ODEs is a fresh perspective. It avoids the pitfalls of inverting videos into the latent space of diffusion models, known to cause structural and temporal inconsistencies.
*   **Spatial Attentive Flow Correction (SAFC)**: This mechanism provides a more direct and controllable way to preserve unedited regions than previous latent space manipulation methods.
*   **Differential Averaging Guidance (DAG)**: While inspired by CFG, DAG adapts this concept to flow steering, offering a clever way to improve semantic alignment without sacrificing structural integrity. The differential signals help refine the editing trajectory efficiently.

**Significance:**

The significance of this work lies in its ability to generate higher-quality, temporally coherent, and structurally consistent edited videos compared to existing training-free methods. This opens up possibilities for more practical and reliable video editing tools.
*   **Improved Editing Quality**: The qualitative and quantitative results clearly show the superiority of FlowDirector in key areas like instruction adherence, temporal consistency, and background preservation.
*   **Practicality**: A training-free approach is inherently more practical as it eliminates the need for expensive and time-consuming fine-tuning for specific editing tasks.
*   **Influence on the Field**: By introducing an alternative to inversion-based methods, FlowDirector has the potential to shift the research focus towards more direct manipulation techniques in video editing. It could inspire new approaches that combine the strengths of both inversion-based and inversion-free methods.

**Strengths:**

*   The paper is well-written and clearly explains the technical details of the proposed framework.
*   The ablation studies provide strong evidence for the effectiveness of the individual components (SAFC and DAG).
*   The quantitative and qualitative results are compelling and demonstrate a clear improvement over existing methods.
*   The paper addresses a significant challenge in text-to-video editing (temporal consistency and structural fidelity) with a novel and effective solution.

**Weaknesses:**

*   The paper acknowledges that FlowDirector might not achieve the highest WarpSSIM score due to its ability to perform more substantial object deformations. It would be helpful to explore alternative metrics that better capture the quality of edits involving significant structural changes.
*   While training-free, the computational cost of directly manipulating video data can still be high. The paper briefly mentions the runtime but could benefit from a more thorough analysis and comparison to other methods.  Also, the appendix mentions the increased computational cost from 4 to 29 minutes (depending on the method used) for editing a 41-frame video.
*   The paper could explore the limitations of the method more thoroughly, e.g., the types of edits that are still challenging for FlowDirector. The impact of the quality of the input text Csrc on the editing result Ctar should be investigated to better understand the limits of the method.

**Score:**

Score: 8

**Rationale:**

FlowDirector represents a significant advancement in text-to-video editing, offering a novel and effective approach that addresses key limitations of existing methods. The inversion-free framework, combined with SAFC and DAG, demonstrates a clear improvement in editing quality, temporal consistency, and structural fidelity. While there are minor weaknesses regarding the computational cost and limited exploration of potential limitations, the overall contribution of the paper is substantial and has the potential to significantly impact the field. The method successfully improves video editing without retraining, offering a practical and adaptable solution.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### TALL -- A Trainable Architecture for Enhancing LLM Performance in Low-Resource Languages
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05057v1)
- **Authors**: Moshe Ofer, Orel Zamler, Amos Azaria
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) excel in high-resource languages but struggle with low-resource languages due to limited training data. This paper presents TALL (Trainable Architecture for Enhancing LLM Performance in Low-Resource Languages), which integrates an LLM with two bilingual translation models. TALL transforms low-resource inputs into high-resource representations, leveraging the LLM's capabilities while preserving linguistic features through dimension alignment layers and custom transformers. Our experiments on Hebrew demonstrate significant improvements over several baselines, including direct use, naive translation, and fine-tuning approaches. The architecture employs a parameter-efficient strategy, freezing pre-trained components while training only lightweight adapter modules, balancing computational efficiency with performance gains.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces TALL (Trainable Architecture for Enhancing LLM Performance in Low-Resource Languages), a novel architecture designed to improve the performance of Large Language Models (LLMs) in low-resource languages. TALL integrates a pre-trained LLM with two bilingual translation models, using dimension alignment adapters and custom transformers to bridge the gap between low- and high-resource language representations.  The architecture is designed to be parameter-efficient, freezing the LLM and translation models and only training the adapter layers. Experiments on Hebrew demonstrate significant improvements over baseline approaches, including direct use of the LLM, naive translation, fine-tuning, and soft prompting. The results suggest that TALL effectively leverages the capabilities of LLMs trained on high-resource languages for low-resource language processing while preserving linguistic features.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a moderately novel architecture that combines pre-trained LLMs with translation models using alignment adapters and custom transformers. The combination of these elements to specifically address low-resource language LLM performance is new, although individual components have been explored in prior research. Dimension alignment, adapter-based approaches, and transfer learning have each been addressed in other works but not combined as elegantly in this context to specifically address the need for LLMs to excel at low resource languages.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a relevant and important problem: the limited performance of LLMs in low-resource languages. LLMs typically need a huge dataset for the language for training. TALL's architecture offers a potential solution by leveraging knowledge from high-resource languages, which can be more easily found. The experimental results on Hebrew are promising, showing significant improvements compared to several baselines. The approach has strong potential for deployment to languages with smaller datasets available. The paper also emphasizes parameter efficiency, which is crucial for practical applications.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The architecture is well-motivated and clearly explained.
    *   The experimental results are convincing, demonstrating consistent improvements over strong baselines.
    *   The parameter-efficient design is a significant advantage.
    *   The evaluation on Hebrew, a morphologically rich language, provides a strong test of the architecture's capabilities.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The single-token learning signal limitation is a significant drawback that prevents conventional LM training. This means there are limitations to the model's ability to learn broader contextual dependencies.
    *   The reliance on translation models introduces a potential source of bias and errors, especially for extremely low-resource languages. While the paper states that the design is not good for extremely low resources, this is still a limitation to the design.
    *   The inference efficiency challenges due to the integration of translation and alignment modules.
    *   The evaluation is limited to a single language (Hebrew), and further validation on other low-resource languages would strengthen the findings.

*   **Impact:** If the approach proves to be generalizable to other low-resource languages, it could have a significant impact on the accessibility and usability of LLMs for a wider range of languages. It potentially provides a model for more inclusive and accessible NLP technologies.

*   **Justification for Score:** The paper presents a novel combination of existing techniques to address a significant problem in NLP. The experimental results are strong, and the architecture is well-designed. However, the limitations regarding error propagation, single-token learning, inference efficiency, and reliance on translation models, and the single language validation somewhat restrict the paper's potential impact.
    The importance of making LLMs useful for low-resource languages is very important to the field of NLP and justifies the high ranking despite its limitations.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### A Survey on Vietnamese Document Analysis and Recognition: Challenges and Future Directions
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05061v1)
- **Authors**: Anh Le, Thanh Lam, Dung Nguyen
- **Abstract**: Vietnamese document analysis and recognition (DAR) is a crucial field with applications in digitization, information retrieval, and automation. Despite advancements in OCR and NLP, Vietnamese text recognition faces unique challenges due to its complex diacritics, tonal variations, and lack of large-scale annotated datasets. Traditional OCR methods often struggle with real-world document variations, while deep learning approaches have shown promise but remain limited by data scarcity and generalization issues. Recently, large language models (LLMs) and vision-language models have demonstrated remarkable improvements in text recognition and document understanding, offering a new direction for Vietnamese DAR. However, challenges such as domain adaptation, multimodal learning, and computational efficiency persist. This survey provide a comprehensive review of existing techniques in Vietnamese document recognition, highlights key limitations, and explores how LLMs can revolutionize the field. We discuss future research directions, including dataset development, model optimization, and the integration of multimodal approaches for improved document intelligence. By addressing these gaps, we aim to foster advancements in Vietnamese DAR and encourage community-driven solutions.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper provides a survey of Vietnamese Document Analysis and Recognition (DAR). It highlights the unique challenges posed by the Vietnamese language (complex diacritics, tonal variations) and the limitations of existing methods, including both traditional OCR techniques and deep learning approaches. The survey discusses datasets, evaluation metrics, and the role of large language models (LLMs) in the field. It concludes by outlining future research directions, such as dataset expansion, model optimization, multimodal learning, and the need for a stronger research community.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Coverage:** The paper offers a good overview of the existing literature in Vietnamese DAR, covering traditional methods, deep learning approaches, and the emerging role of LLMs.
    *   **Specific Focus:** The paper clearly articulates the specific challenges associated with the Vietnamese language, emphasizing the complexities of diacritics, tonal variations, and the lack of sufficient datasets.
    *   **Future Directions:** The suggested future research directions (dataset development, multimodal approaches, model optimization) are relevant and well-justified.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Depth of LLM Discussion:** While the paper mentions LLMs, it doesn't delve deeply into specific LLM architectures or fine-tuning strategies tailored for Vietnamese DAR. It highlights the potential but lacks concrete details on implementation.
    *   **Evaluation of Existing Works:** The survey lacks a consistent comparative analysis of the accuracy or efficiency of the reviewed methodologies for specific Vietnamese OCR datasets, which would've significantly augmented the paper's utility.
    *   **Novelty:** While it's a good survey, the paper doesn't present any novel techniques or insights. It primarily summarizes existing work.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    The primary value of this paper lies in its comprehensive aggregation of information related to a somewhat niche but important area of OCR.  It consolidates research that is, otherwise, spread across various conferences and publications. However, given the survey nature of the paper, the novelty is limited. Other surveys in document analysis exist, and this paper provides a specific view onto the Vietnamese language. The value stems from putting all the information in one place for researchers to start exploring the field, and highlighting the pain-points and future directions. It serves to promote the specific area and increase its visibility to the general public interested in OCR and document understanding. It doesn’t advance the state of the art *per se*, but helps in understanding the current status and directs future investigations.

*   **Potential Influence:**
    *   The paper could serve as a valuable starting point for researchers new to the field of Vietnamese DAR.
    *   It could encourage the development of standardized datasets and evaluation metrics.
    *   It could stimulate further research into the application of LLMs to Vietnamese DAR.
    *   It can serve as a consolidated document of current pain points, encouraging new researchers to get involved.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the strengths and weaknesses, the paper is a solid survey that provides a useful overview of the field. However, it lacks substantial novelty in methodology or approach. The survey nature of the paper is also its limit, considering the high level of research expected of journals nowadays. Therefore, I believe a score of 6 is appropriate, reflecting the solid foundation the paper provides and its potential for future impact without groundbreaking advancements.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### Does It Make Sense to Speak of Introspection in Large Language Models?
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05068v1)
- **Authors**: Iulia Comşa, Murray Shanahan
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) exhibit compelling linguistic behaviour, and sometimes offer self-reports, that is to say statements about their own nature, inner workings, or behaviour. In humans, such reports are often attributed to a faculty of introspection and are typically linked to consciousness. This raises the question of how to interpret self-reports produced by LLMs, given their increasing linguistic fluency and cognitive capabilities. To what extent (if any) can the concept of introspection be meaningfully applied to LLMs? Here, we present and critique two examples of apparent introspective self-report from LLMs. In the first example, an LLM attempts to describe the process behind its own ``creative'' writing, and we argue this is not a valid example of introspection. In the second example, an LLM correctly infers the value of its own temperature parameter, and we argue that this can be legitimately considered a minimal example of introspection, albeit one that is (presumably) not accompanied by conscious experience.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores the concept of introspection in Large Language Models (LLMs). It argues that while LLMs can generate self-reports (statements about their own nature), not all such reports qualify as genuine introspection.  The authors propose a lightweight definition of introspection for LLMs: an LLM self-report is introspective if it accurately describes an internal state (or mechanism) *through* a causal process linking that internal state to the self-report. They present two case studies: (1) an LLM describing its creative process (deemed not valid introspection because it appears to be mimicry of human introspective reports), and (2) an LLM estimating its own temperature parameter (argued to be a minimal but valid example of introspection because of the direct causal connection between the parameter and the report). The paper emphasizes the importance of causal connections between internal states and self-reports to avoid anthropomorphizing LLMs and to provide a clearer understanding of their capabilities.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its attempt to rigorously define and apply the concept of introspection to LLMs. While the idea of LLM self-reports has been explored, this paper provides a specific, arguably more defensible, definition based on causal connections, distinguishing it from simpler notions of mimicry. The two case studies, while not empirically exhaustive, are useful for illustrating the application of the proposed definition. The exploration of temperature estimation as a potentially legitimate form of LLM introspection, distinct from simple imitation of human-style introspection, is a worthwhile contribution.

*   **Significance:** The significance is moderate. The paper contributes to the ongoing debate about consciousness and self-awareness in AI, particularly LLMs. The rigorous definition offered helps to move beyond simplistic anthropomorphism and encourages a more nuanced analysis of LLM capabilities. By establishing a clear framework, the paper provides a foundation for future research, potentially guiding the design of experiments and development of policies surrounding AI. The paper also points out that a concept that is often bundled into the term "introspection," multi-step cognitive processes versus the actual phenomenal experience, should be separated for clarity when studying introspection and AI.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear definition of introspection tailored to LLMs.
    *   Emphasis on causal connections as a key criterion for valid introspection.
    *   Case studies illustrating the application of the proposed definition.
    *   Well-argued distinction between mimicry and genuine (albeit minimal) introspection.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The case studies are limited and primarily illustrative rather than empirically conclusive. More robust experimentation could strengthen the claims.
    *   The definition of "internal state" could benefit from further clarification. The boundary between internal and external can be blurry even in humans, so more discussion in the context of LLMs would be helpful.
    *   The paper acknowledges the mitigation strategies for the potential issues pertaining to entity continuity, but the risk still remains.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper can influence the way researchers and policymakers think about self-awareness in LLMs. It encourages a more cautious and scientifically grounded approach to interpreting LLM self-reports, preventing over-interpretation of their capabilities. It can also inspire more targeted research into specific mechanisms underlying LLM behavior that might qualify as genuine forms of introspection.

*   **Justification for Score:** The paper is a valuable contribution that offers a more nuanced and rigorous way of thinking about introspection in LLMs. However, its limitations in terms of empirical validation and the inherent challenges in defining "internal states" prevent it from being a truly groundbreaking work.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Reason-to-Recommend: Using Interaction-of-Thought Reasoning to Enhance LLM Recommendation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05069v1)
- **Authors**: Keyu Zhao, Fengli Xu, Yong Li
- **Abstract**: Driven by advances in Large Language Models (LLMs), integrating them into recommendation tasks has gained interest due to their strong semantic understanding and prompt flexibility. Prior work encoded user-item interactions or metadata into prompts for recommendations. In parallel, LLM reasoning, boosted by test-time scaling and reinforcement learning, has excelled in fields like mathematics and code, where reasoning traces and correctness signals are clear, enabling high performance and interpretability. However, directly applying these reasoning methods to recommendation is ineffective because user feedback is implicit and lacks reasoning supervision. To address this, we propose $\textbf{R2Rec}$, a reasoning-enhanced recommendation framework that samples interaction chains from the user-item graph and converts them into structured interaction-of-thoughts via a progressive masked prompting strategy, with each thought representing stepwise reasoning grounded in interaction context. This allows LLMs to simulate step-by-step decision-making based on implicit patterns. We design a two-stage training pipeline: supervised fine-tuning teaches basic reasoning from high-quality traces, and reinforcement learning refines reasoning via reward signals, alleviating sparse explicit supervision. Experiments on three real-world datasets show R2Rec outperforms classical and LLM-based baselines with an average $\textbf{10.48%}$ improvement in HitRatio@1 and $\textbf{131.81%}$ gain over the original LLM. Furthermore, the explicit reasoning chains enhance interpretability by revealing the decision process. Our code is available at: https://anonymous.4open.science/r/R2Rec-7C5D.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces R2Rec, a reasoning-enhanced recommendation framework leveraging Large Language Models (LLMs). It addresses the challenge of applying LLM reasoning techniques to recommendation tasks, which lack explicit reasoning data and supervision. R2Rec constructs "interaction chains" from user-item interaction graphs and transforms them into structured "interaction-of-thoughts" using a masked prompting strategy.  A two-stage training pipeline (supervised fine-tuning (SFT) followed by reinforcement learning (RL)) is used to improve the LLM's recommendation capabilities by learning stepwise decision-making based on implicit interaction patterns. The approach aims to improve both recommendation accuracy and interpretability. The results demonstrate improved performance compared to classical and LLM-based baselines and offer more interpretable reasoning chains.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper makes several novel contributions. The core idea of transforming user-item interactions into explicit, structured reasoning chains suitable for LLMs is a significant step. The progressive, masked prompting strategy for creating "interaction-of-thoughts" appears to be a novel approach to eliciting stepwise reasoning from LLMs within the recommendation domain.  The two-stage SFT+RL training pipeline is also well-motivated and addresses the scarcity of explicit reasoning data. While LLM reasoning and prompt engineering are active research areas, applying them to the specific problem of recommendation using this particular interaction chain representation is a distinctive contribution.

**Significance:**  The paper addresses a crucial gap in the application of LLMs to recommendation systems. While LLMs have shown promise, directly leveraging their reasoning capabilities has been challenging due to the implicit nature of user feedback. R2Rec offers a structured approach to introduce reasoning, which can improve accuracy and interpretability. The performance gains reported, especially the average improvement of 10.48% in HitRatio@1 over strong baselines, are practically significant. The enhanced interpretability provided by the explicit reasoning chains is also valuable, as it can provide insights into the model's decision-making process and potentially improve user trust.  The ablation studies provide strong evidence for the effectiveness of the individual components of the framework. The transfer learning results further strengthen the significance.

**Strengths:**

*   **Well-Motivated Problem:** The paper clearly identifies and motivates a relevant problem.
*   **Novel Approach:** R2Rec presents a novel framework for injecting structured reasoning into LLM-based recommendation.
*   **Strong Empirical Results:** The experiments are comprehensive, using multiple real-world datasets and comparing against strong baselines. The performance gains are significant.
*   **Ablation Studies:** Thorough ablation studies demonstrate the importance of each component of the proposed framework.
*   **Interpretability:** The paper emphasizes and demonstrates the improved interpretability of the model's recommendations.
*   **Reproducibility:** The authors provide open-source code, which facilitates reproducibility and further research.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Computational Cost:** The use of LLMs and the two-stage training pipeline can be computationally expensive. The paper mentions the hardware used, but further discussion of the training and inference costs (e.g., training time, inference latency) would be beneficial for practical deployment considerations.
*   **Context Length Limitations:** As the authors acknowledge, the context length of LLMs limits the number of interaction-of-thought sequences that can be processed simultaneously. This could restrict the model's ability to consider a wider range of interactions.
*   **Prompt Sensitivity:**  LLM performance can be highly sensitive to prompt design.  While the paper describes the prompting strategy, a more detailed analysis of the impact of different prompt variations could be included.
*   **Scalability:** While the paper describes the interaction chains as enabling scalability and overcomes the implicit nature of user feedback, the performance of the framework over much larger graphs are untested.

**Overall:**

R2Rec is a well-designed and thoroughly evaluated framework for enhancing LLM-based recommendation with structured reasoning. The novelty of the interaction chain representation and prompting strategy, combined with the strong empirical results and interpretability benefits, makes this a valuable contribution to the field. The limitations regarding computational cost and context length are acknowledged and represent potential areas for future research.

**Score: 8**

**Justification:** The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of recommender systems by effectively integrating structured reasoning into LLM-based models. The approach demonstrably improves performance and interpretability. While the computational cost and context length limitations are valid concerns, the overall impact of the work warrants a high score. The paper has the potential to influence future research directions in this area.

- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 8/10

### RIVAL: Reinforcement Learning with Iterative and Adversarial Optimization for Machine Translation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05070v1)
- **Authors**: Tianjiao Li, Mengran Yu, Chenyu Shi, Yanjun Zhao, Xiaojing Liu, Qiang Zhang, Qi Zhang, Xuanjing Huang, Jiayin Wang
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) possess strong multilingual capabilities, and combining Reinforcement Learning from Human Feedback (RLHF) with translation tasks has shown great potential. However, we observe that this paradigm performs unexpectedly poorly when applied to colloquial subtitle translation tasks. In this work, we investigate this issue and find that the offline reward model (RM) gradually diverges from the online LLM due to distributional shift, ultimately leading to undesirable training outcomes. To address this, we propose RIVAL, an adversarial training framework that formulates the process as a min-max game between the RM and the LLM. RIVAL iteratively updates the both models, with the RM trained to distinguish strong from weak translations (qualitative preference reward), and the LLM trained to enhance its translation for closing this gap. To stabilize training and improve generalizability, we also incorporate quantitative preference reward (e.g., BLEU) into the RM, enabling reference-free quality modeling aligned with human evaluation. Through extensive experiments, we demonstrate that the proposed adversarial training framework significantly improves upon translation baselines.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of suboptimal performance of Reinforcement Learning from Human Feedback (RLHF) when applied to colloquial subtitle translation. The authors identify that the offline reward model (RM) diverges from the online LLM during training due to distributional shift, leading to reward hacking and undesirable outcomes. To counteract this, they propose RIVAL, an adversarial training framework that formulates the training process as a min-max game between the RM and the LLM. RIVAL iteratively updates both models, training the RM to distinguish strong from weak translations (qualitative preference reward) and the LLM to close the gap between its translations and those of strong translators. They also incorporate quantitative preference reward (e.g., BLEU) into the RM to stabilize training and improve generalization. The paper presents experimental results demonstrating the effectiveness of RIVAL compared to baseline translation models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of using adversarial training in conjunction with RLHF for machine translation is reasonably novel. Most prior work focuses on either directly applying RLHF or using supervised fine-tuning. The formulation of the RM and LLM training as a min-max game and iterative updating to mitigate distributional shift addresses a significant practical issue. The introduction of both qualitative and quantitative preference rewards, and training a single RM for both, adds to the novelty.

*   **Significance:** The paper tackles a challenging problem in applying RLHF to a specific type of NMT, which can have potentially broad impacts. The identification of distributional shift as a key reason for the failure of vanilla RLHF is valuable. Demonstrating improvements in a real-world, challenging task (colloquial subtitle translation) adds practical significance. The exploration of MAE as an alternative loss function compared to MSE for the reward model is also a valuable practical insight.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies and articulates the problem of applying standard RLHF techniques to a challenging task.
    *   **Well-Motivated Solution:** The proposed RIVAL framework is well-motivated by the distributional shift issue and draws inspiration from established adversarial training techniques.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The experiments cover both in-domain and out-of-domain translation scenarios, and the use of GPT-40 as a judge adds rigor to the evaluation, although the inherent limitations of LLM-based evaluation should be acknowledged.
    *   **Practical Insights:** The paper provides useful insights into the selection of loss functions (MAE vs. MSE) for the reward model and discusses the importance of balancing qualitative and quantitative rewards.
    *   **Opensource Dataset and Code:** Making the dataset and code available is an important contribution to the community and will facilitate further research.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Task Specificity:** The approach may be tailored to colloquial subtitle translation and might not directly generalize to other NMT tasks or broader applications of RLHF. The paper could benefit from exploring generalizability to other domains.
    *   **LLM-based Evaluation:** While using GPT-40 for evaluation provides an automated and scalable solution, the trustworthiness of GPT-40 as a judge is not well-understood. Providing human evaluations of the improvement would strengthen the evaluation and provide greater credibility.
    *   **Ablation Studies:** The paper lacks detailed ablation studies to determine the contributions of each component of the RIVAL framework. More specifically, what is the impact of using only qualitative reward, or only quantitative reward and etc? How does each individual component impact the final result?
    *   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:** The paper does not explicitly discuss the sensitivity of the RIVAL framework to different hyperparameters, particularly those related to the balance between qualitative and quantitative rewards, the learning rates for the RM and LLM, and the size of the replay buffer.
    *   **Computational Cost:** The computational cost of the iterative adversarial training is likely to be significantly higher than standard RLHF. This aspect is not adequately addressed.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence research on applying RLHF to specialized translation tasks and general post-training scenarios, highlighting the importance of mitigating distributional shift. The use of adversarial training could inspire new approaches for stabilizing RLHF in other domains.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a novel and well-motivated solution to a practical problem. It provides empirical evidence demonstrating the effectiveness of the proposed approach. However, the task specificity, dependence on LLM-based evaluations, lack of extensive ablation studies, computational cost, and limitations on hyperparameter sensitivity hold back its true potential.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Just a Scratch: Enhancing LLM Capabilities for Self-harm Detection through Intent Differentiation and Emoji Interpretation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05073v1)
- **Authors**: Soumitra Ghosh, Gopendra Vikram Singh, Shambhavi, Sabarna Choudhury, Asif Ekbal
- **Abstract**: Self-harm detection on social media is critical for early intervention and mental health support, yet remains challenging due to the subtle, context-dependent nature of such expressions. Identifying self-harm intent aids suicide prevention by enabling timely responses, but current large language models (LLMs) struggle to interpret implicit cues in casual language and emojis. This work enhances LLMs' comprehension of self-harm by distinguishing intent through nuanced language-emoji interplay. We present the Centennial Emoji Sensitivity Matrix (CESM-100), a curated set of 100 emojis with contextual self-harm interpretations and the Self-Harm Identification aNd intent Extraction with Supportive emoji sensitivity (SHINES) dataset, offering detailed annotations for self-harm labels, casual mentions (CMs), and serious intents (SIs). Our unified framework: a) enriches inputs using CESM-100; b) fine-tunes LLMs for multi-task learning: self-harm detection (primary) and CM/SI span detection (auxiliary); c) generates explainable rationales for self-harm predictions. We evaluate the framework on three state-of-the-art LLMs-Llama 3, Mental-Alpaca, and MentalLlama, across zero-shot, few-shot, and fine-tuned scenarios. By coupling intent differentiation with contextual cues, our approach commendably enhances LLM performance in both detection and explanation tasks, effectively addressing the inherent ambiguity in self-harm signals. The SHINES dataset, CESM-100 and codebase are publicly available at: https://www.iitp.ac.in/~ai-nlp-ml/resources.html#SHINES .
- **Summary**: This paper introduces a novel approach to self-harm detection on social media using Large Language Models (LLMs). The key idea is to enhance LLMs' ability to differentiate between casual mentions and serious intent by incorporating contextual emoji analysis. The authors present two main contributions: the Centennial Emoji Sensitivity Matrix (CESM-100), a curated set of emojis with contextual interpretations relevant to self-harm, and the SHINES dataset, a new dataset with annotations for self-harm labels, casual mentions, serious intent, and emoji interpretations. The proposed framework fine-tunes LLMs for multi-task learning, including self-harm detection, span extraction, and rationale generation. The framework is evaluated across zero-shot, few-shot, and fine-tuned scenarios using Llama 3, Mental-Alpaca, and MentalLlama. The results demonstrate that the proposed approach enhances LLM performance in both detection and explanation tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper introduces a novel approach by explicitly focusing on differentiating casual mentions and serious intent in self-harm detection using LLMs. The CESM-100 and SHINES dataset are valuable resources for the community. The multi-task learning framework is well-designed to leverage shared knowledge across tasks.
*   **Clarity and Organization:** The paper is well-written and organized, making it easy to follow the proposed approach and experimental setup.
*   **Empirical Evaluation:** The paper presents a comprehensive evaluation across different LLMs and experimental settings, providing strong evidence for the effectiveness of the proposed approach. The ablation study helps to understand the impact of CESM-100 and multi-task fine-tuning.
*   **Reproducibility:** The authors provide the SHINES dataset, CESM-100 and codebase, promoting reproducibility and further research in this area.
*   **Focus on explainability**: The paper focuses on explainability by generating rationale which is crucial in mental health domains.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Dataset Scope:** The reliance on Reddit data may introduce platform bias and limit the generalizability of the findings to other social media platforms. The size of the SHINES dataset (5206 posts) is relatively small compared to other datasets used for LLM training, which could limit the performance gains.
*   **Model Choices:** Although the model choices were guided by hardware constraints, larger proprietary models might show better performance.
*   **Limited Error Analysis:** While a qualitative analysis is provided, a more detailed error analysis would have been beneficial to understand the specific challenges faced by the models and to identify areas for future improvement.
*   **Overclaimed Significance**: Results obtained by prompting is close to fine-tuned results especially when leveraging few-shot learning.

**Significance:**

The paper addresses an important problem with significant societal impact. Self-harm detection is crucial for early intervention and mental health support. The paper's contributions have the potential to improve the accuracy and reliability of automated self-harm detection systems, enabling timely assistance to individuals in need. The emphasis on explainability makes the approach more trustworthy.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign a score of **7** based on the following rationale:

The paper's novelty lies in the integration of contextual emoji analysis and intent differentiation within a multi-task learning framework for self-harm detection. The creation of CESM-100 and SHINES dataset is valuable, and the experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed approach. The focus on explainability is a significant strength.

However, the paper also has some limitations. The dataset size and reliance on Reddit data may limit the generalizability of the findings. A more detailed error analysis would have strengthened the paper. While the improvements are appreciable with limited compute resources, larger pre-trained models or other state-of-the-art methodologies might yield more significant gains. These limitations prevent the paper from achieving a higher score.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### SeedEdit 3.0: Fast and High-Quality Generative Image Editing
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05083v1)
- **Authors**: Peng Wang, Yichun Shi, Xiaochen Lian, Zhonghua Zhai, Xin Xia, Xuefeng Xiao, Weilin Huang, Jianchao Yang
- **Abstract**: We introduce SeedEdit 3.0, in companion with our T2I model Seedream 3.0 [22], which significantly improves over our previous version [27] in both aspects of edit instruction following and image content (e.g., ID/IP) preservation on real image inputs. Additional to model upgrading with T2I, in this report, we present several key improvements. First, we develop an enhanced data curation pipeline with a meta-info paradigm and meta-info embedding strategy that help mix images from multiple data sources. This allows us to scale editing data effectively, and meta information is helpfult to connect VLM with diffusion model more closely. Second, we introduce a joint learning pipeline for computing a diffusion loss and a reward loss. Finally, we evaluate SeedEdit 3.0 on our testing benchmarks, for real image editing, where it achieves a best trade-off between multiple aspects, yielding a high usability rate of 56.1%, compared to SeedEdit 1.6 (38.4%), GPT4o (37.1%) and Gemini 2.0 (30.3%).
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the SeedEdit 3.0 paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SeedEdit 3.0, a generative image editing system developed by ByteDance, building upon their previous SeedEdit versions and the Seedream T2I model. SeedEdit 3.0 aims for improved instruction following and content preservation in real image editing. Key improvements include an enhanced data curation pipeline leveraging a meta-info paradigm and meta-info embedding to handle diverse data sources, and a joint learning pipeline with diffusion and reward losses. The system is evaluated on internal benchmarks, demonstrating a better trade-off between instruction adherence, content preservation, and image quality compared to SeedEdit 1.6, GPT-40, and Gemini 2.0, while also achieving faster inference speeds.  The method uses a multi-granularity label strategy to combine different sources of image editing data effectively, with different levels of labels: data-level task label, text-level recaption, and pixel-level tagging. The models used in the method include a VLM (Vision Language Model) at the bottom and a causal diffusion network on top.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper demonstrates incremental advancements rather than a completely revolutionary approach. While the individual components like the meta-info driven data curation and the joint diffusion/reward loss training are not entirely novel on their own, their specific combination and application within the SeedEdit framework is interesting. The introduction of meta-information embeddings to better integrate VLM understanding with diffusion model generation is a solid contribution. The unified approach to data curation and training (real & synthetic images, using reward models) is well executed.

*   **Significance:** The significance lies in the practical improvements achieved in a real-world image editing product. The improvements to usability rate and speed compared to commercial competitors like GPT-40 and Gemini 2.0 are noteworthy, suggesting the practical impact of the described techniques. The focus on real image editing, addressing the gap between synthetic data training and real-world application, is a valuable direction. However, the evaluation is conducted primarily on internal datasets and benchmarks, limiting generalizability. Public datasets and benchmarks would strengthen the claims.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Systematic approach: The paper clearly outlines the individual improvements made to the SeedEdit framework.
    *   Practical focus: The work is driven by the goal of improving a real-world image editing product.
    *   Performance gains: The reported improvements in usability and speed are significant.
    *   Comprehensive data curation and training pipeline.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Limited novelty:  The individual techniques are not entirely novel on their own.
    *   Internal benchmarks:  The evaluation relies heavily on internal datasets and benchmarks, which affects the reliability of the claims.
    *   Lack of ablation studies:  Ablation studies to demonstrate the impact of each individual component would be valuable.
    *   While it does provide some comparison with GPT40, the information lacks specifics of implementation (ex. specific model used) which might be valuable.

*   **Potential Influence:**  The paper can influence the direction of research in generative image editing by highlighting the importance of data curation and training techniques that bridge the gap between synthetic training and real-world application. The meta-info embedding strategy could be adopted in other similar frameworks. The industrial backing of ByteDance gives the work a higher chance of influencing practical applications.

**Justification of Score:**

While the paper shows solid engineering and significant improvements in a practical setting, its novelty is incremental.  The improvements are well-engineered and lead to measurable gains. The limited generalizability because of the closed datasets keeps the rating from being higher. Public datasets or more reproducible benchmarks would be valuable.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### Astraea: A GPU-Oriented Token-wise Acceleration Framework for Video Diffusion Transformers
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05096v1)
- **Authors**: Haosong Liu, Yuge Cheng, Zihan Liu, Aiyue Chen, Yiwu Yao, Chen Chen, Jingwen Leng, Yu Feng, Minyi Guo
- **Abstract**: Video diffusion transformers (vDiTs) have made impressive progress in text-to-video generation, but their high computational demands present major challenges for practical deployment. While existing acceleration methods reduce workload at various granularities, they often rely on heuristics, limiting their applicability. We introduce ASTRAEA, an automatic framework that searches for near-optimal configurations for vDiT-based video generation. At its core, ASTRAEA proposes a lightweight token selection mechanism and a memory-efficient, GPU-parallel sparse attention strategy, enabling linear reductions in execution time with minimal impact on generation quality. To determine optimal token reduction for different timesteps, we further design a search framework that leverages a classic evolutionary algorithm to automatically determine the distribution of the token budget effectively. Together, ASTRAEA achieves up to 2.4x inference speedup on a single GPU with great scalability (up to 13.2x speedup on 8 GPUs) while retaining better video quality compared to the state-of-the-art methods (<0.5% loss on the VBench score compared to the baseline vDiT models).
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "ASTRAEA: A GPU-Oriented Token-wise Acceleration Framework for Video Diffusion Transformers":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ASTRAEA, a framework designed to accelerate video diffusion transformers (vDiTs) for text-to-video generation. ASTRAEA operates at the token level, using a lightweight token selection mechanism and a GPU-efficient sparse attention strategy to reduce computation. It also features a search framework based on an evolutionary algorithm to find optimal token budget allocation across different denoising timesteps. The authors claim significant inference speedups (up to 2.4x on a single GPU and 13.2x on 8 GPUs) with minimal impact on video quality (less than 0.5% loss on VBench score) compared to baseline vDiT models and existing acceleration methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in the combination of token-level selection, GPU-friendly sparse attention, and an evolutionary search strategy for token budget allocation. While token selection and sparse attention have been explored before, ASTRAEA attempts to address their limitations concerning memory overhead and computational inaccuracies on GPUs. The use of an evolutionary algorithm for token budget distribution also represents a departure from purely heuristic-based approaches. However, it is debatable how "lightweight" the token selection is, considering the need for evolutionary search.

*   **Significance:** The claimed performance improvements are significant, potentially making vDiTs more practical for deployment. The focus on GPU optimization is also relevant, given the prevalence of GPUs in video generation workflows. The VBench results, if robust and independently verified, demonstrate a reasonable trade-off between speed and quality. The paper also attempts to address the practical problem of automating hyperparameter tuning. However, the approach of using evolutionary algorithms has a high computational overhead, and thus it would be interesting to see how it scales with larger models and datasets.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper tackles a real-world problem.
    *   Provides a clear description of the ASTRAEA framework.
    *   Presents quantitative results across different models.
    *   Focus on GPU optimization is practically relevant.
    *   Ablation studies and sensitivity analysis provides insights into the impact of different design choices.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited novelty on the token selection method**: Although the paper indicates that the token selection is lightweight, the mechanism is largely inspired by existing work like Toca[43], which the paper references heavily.
    *   **Evolutionary Algorithm Limitations:** Evolutionary Algorithms (EAs) often struggle with high-dimensional optimization problems. Despite the efforts made to streamline the search space, there may be alternative optimization techniques that offer better scalability and convergence.
    *   **Search Cost:** A significant search time (82 GPU hours on 8 A100 GPUs) is required to find the optimal configuration using evolutionary algorithms. This high computational cost may limit the applicability of the framework, particularly for rapid prototyping and model development. The evolutionary algorithm is the main bottleneck, and it would be interesting to see how well it performs when applied on much larger video generation models.
    *   **Overclaiming**: The paper overclaims on its novelty as many aspects of the proposed ASTRAEA is inspired by existing work.
    *   **Limited Evaluation**: The method needs to be compared with more existing methods.

*   **Potential Impact:** ASTRAEA has the potential to influence the development of more efficient video generation systems, particularly for GPU-based deployments. By demonstrating the feasibility of token-level optimization and automated budget allocation, it could inspire further research in this area. However, its practical adoption depends on reducing the search cost and addressing the generalizability concerns.

**Justification:**

ASTRAEA attempts to make vDiTs more practical by automating the hyperparameter tuning aspect, with novel combination of token-level selection, GPU-friendly sparse attention, and an evolutionary search strategy for token budget allocation. However, the lack of a more compelling approach to improve over many existing algorithms, high search cost, and overclaiming limitations, limit its significance.
